## Slice 72: current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M REST batch order modification

`PUT /fapi/v1/batchOrders` and `PUT /dapi/v1/batchOrders` were audited independently against their live canonical Trade sections, the COIN-M/UM architecture-integration notice fully effective by 2026-06-30, the combined derivatives changelog change effective 2026-07-30, and official generated JavaScript connector HEAD `092e4f2`. Both are signed TRADE mutations accepting a required batch of at most five LIMIT-order amendments. Every item requires nonblank `symbol`, a BUY/SELL `side`, a complete positive `quantity`, a complete positive `price`, and either int64 `orderId` or `origClientOrderId`; `orderId` takes precedence when both identifiers are supplied. Optional int64 `modifyId` is passed through without uniqueness validation and is returned only when supplied. The amendments execute concurrently while response elements retain request-list order, and an element-level failure must not discard successful siblings. Both products publish IP weight 5, and the outer `recvWindow` is capped at 60,000 milliseconds.

The implementations now materialize the input exactly once, reject null, empty, over-five, null-item, missing-identifier, invalid-side, blank-symbol/client-identifier, non-positive amendment, and unusable `priceMatch` requests before transport, and preserve precise numeric JSON for decimal and int64 fields inside the signed `batchOrders` form value. The transport injects one outer timestamp and keeps the established signature/query split. The response remains an ordered `List<CallResult<BinanceFuturesOrder>>`, so a Binance error object becomes a failed item at the same index while valid order acknowledgements retain current fields including conditional `modifyId`. Transport-level failures now return an empty typed data collection instead of a null-forgiven placeholder.

Binance's current batch sources contain several contradictions, so the wrapper deliberately does not invent a permissive wire shape. USDⓈ-M publishes `priceMatch` while simultaneously requiring `price` and prohibiting their combination; COIN-M does not publish `priceMatch` for this route. Both therefore reject non-null `priceMatch` fail-closed. The USDⓈ-M generated item schema also lists `stopPrice`, although the endpoint is expressly limited to LIMIT amendments and delegates its parameter rules to single Modify Order, which has no stop-price field; it is not exposed. The live/generated batch schemas duplicate `timestamp` and `recvWindow` inside items as well as at the outer signed request, while the COIN-M example itself omits identifiers, quantity, and price that the current migration contract requires. Per-item timing fields are therefore treated as generator/catalog artifacts and not emitted. The pages require `batchOrders` and cap it at five without spelling out a minimum; the wrapper rejects an empty collection fail-closed because it contains no amendment, and the connector's empty-array examples are not treated as stronger contract evidence. Finally, COIN-M's isolated batch wording for `origClientOrderId` conflicts with the current new-order identifier pattern and single-modify contract; the client enforces nonblank identity but does not add an incompatible undocumented character restriction.

Three no-network tests cover both signed PUT paths, form/query split, API-key authentication, exact IP weights, one-to-five bounds, numeric decimal/int64 JSON, identifier combinations, conditional `modifyId`, current response data, mixed success/error ordering, explicit/configured receive-window rejection, and every local item validation. The complete suite now has 243 passing tests, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request was sent.

## Slice 73: current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M REST order-modification history

`GET /fapi/v1/orderAmendment` and `GET /dapi/v1/orderAmendment` were rechecked independently against their live canonical Trade sections, the combined derivatives changelog change effective 2026-07-30, and official generated JavaScript connector HEAD `092e4f2`. Both are signed USER_DATA query endpoints with IP weight 1. They require nonblank `symbol` plus either int64 `orderId` or `origClientOrderId`; both may be sent and `orderId` takes precedence. Optional `startTime` and `endTime` are inclusive modification-time boundaries. The server default limit is 50 and the published maximum is 100, `recvWindow` is capped at 60,000 milliseconds, and modification history older than three months is unavailable.

Both implementations now reject missing/blank symbols, missing identities, blank supplied client identities, limits above 100, and explicit or configured receive windows above 60,000 before transport. The public parameter is renamed from the imprecise `clientOrderId` to the wire-accurate `origClientOrderId`. All filters remain in the signed GET query and timestamp/signature injection remains transport-owned. Binance publishes neither a minimum limit nor a maximum query-window span for these routes, and describes old history as unavailable rather than an invalid request; the wrapper therefore does not invent a positive-limit, time-order, three-month-relative-clock, or range-length rejection. A caller can use the inclusive time filters, while the server remains authoritative for retention.

The current response shape is shared across products: int64 amendment and order identifiers, symbol, pair, client order id, modification time, and an `amendment` object containing decimal before/after price and original-quantity values, an int64 modification count, and nullable int64 `modifyId`. The latter is nested inside `amendment` and appears only for modifications whose request supplied it. The stale wrapper-only top-level `priceMatch` property was removed. The live COIN-M page and generated COIN-M type incorrectly describe `amendment.count` as a 24-hour trade count; its location and field name, the USDⓈ-M endpoint/type definition, and both examples show that this is copied ticker text and the value is the order-modification count. That evidence-backed interpretation is the implemented meaning.

Four no-network tests cover both signed GET paths, API-key authentication, exact weight, both identifier combinations and precision, inclusive time serialization, default-limit omission, maximum limit, receive-window ceilings, complete shared response data, nested present/absent `modifyId`, int64/decimal deserialization, and all explicit local validation. The complete suite now has 247 passing tests, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request was sent.

## Slice 74: current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M WebSocket API order modification

USDⓈ-M and COIN-M WebSocket API `order.modify` were rechecked independently against their live canonical Trade sections, the combined derivatives changelog change effective 2026-07-30, and official generated JavaScript connector HEAD `092e4f2`. Both are signed `order.modify` requests sent to their product WebSocket API addresses through `ws-fapi/v1` and `ws-dapi/v1`. They amend existing LIMIT orders and move a successful amendment to the back of the match queue. Both require nonblank `symbol`, BUY/SELL `side`, a complete positive numeric `quantity`, a complete positive numeric `price`, timestamp, API key, signature, and either int64 `orderId` or `origClientOrderId`; Binance permits both identities and gives `orderId` precedence. Optional int64 `modifyId` is passed through without uniqueness validation and returned only when supplied. An order is eligible for fewer than 10,000 modifications. Filter failures reject the amendment without changing the order; a partially filled order is cancelled when the new quantity is less than or equal to its executed quantity, and a GTX order is cancelled when its new price would execute immediately. These state-dependent rules are documented publicly and remain server-authoritative rather than being guessed from unavailable local order state.

The public methods now make `price` mandatory, expose `modifyId`, preserve numeric decimal and int64 JSON values instead of narrowing them to formatted strings, validate required values and identities before opening a connection, and apply the configured receive window through a new int64 normalization overload. The previous COIN-M surface narrowed `recvWindow` to int32 even though the current schema and connector permit int64; it now preserves the published type. COIN-M explicitly caps `recvWindow` at 60,000 milliseconds, including the configured default, while the current USDⓈ-M WebSocket Modify Order section publishes no such ceiling. The wrapper therefore does not copy the REST or COIN-M ceiling into USDⓈ-M. Public links, return guidance, README, and console examples now point at and compile against the current required-price and optional-`modifyId` contract.

The same unresolved `priceMatch` contradiction found on the REST operation exists in both WebSocket API sources: `price` is mandatory while `priceMatch` is published as optional and prohibited with `price`. The generated connector exposes both without resolving the combination. Non-null `priceMatch` therefore remains visible but is rejected fail-closed before transport. The wrapper does not emit a live financial mutation whose own current contract says it is invalid.

USDⓈ-M publishes IP weight 0 plus one order on each 10-second and one-minute order counter; COIN-M publishes IP weight 1 plus one on each order counter. The endpoint calls now carry the exact product IP-weight metadata, correcting USDⓈ-M from 1 to 0. Brutal limitation: the existing Futures WebSocket query transport accepts a `weight` argument but does not consume it in a client-side limiter, and it has no order-counter limiter. It still enforces the connection message rate and shared server-directed 418/429 guard. Although the transport deserializes Binance's rate-limit envelope, it returns only the operation result and neither exposes nor consumes the returned counters. These per-request weight values are therefore metadata rather than local pre-request throttling. Adding a multi-dimensional limiter only for these two methods would be false safety and a systemic transport expansion, so it remains an explicit forward-review item rather than being hidden inside this endpoint slice.

Four no-network tests lock both method/path/weight contracts, exact numeric request fields, identifier and `modifyId` precision beyond JavaScript's safe integer range, product-specific receive-window behavior, all local validation, fail-closed `priceMatch`, complete signed-response envelope deserialization, current product response differences, and conditional present/absent `modifyId`. The complete suite now has 251 passing tests. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

2. Perform mandatory Backward Review 16 across Slices 71-75, including an explicit decision on the unconsumed Futures WebSocket weight metadata, before another implementation slice.
3. Re-rank the 2026-08-05 All Orders and Force Orders response changes after Review 16.

## Slice 75: current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M `ORDER_TRADE_UPDATE` event contracts

The two `ORDER_TRADE_UPDATE` user-data events were compared field by field against their live official schemas, the combined derivatives changelog change effective 2026-07-30, and official generated JavaScript connector HEAD `092e4f2`. The changelog addition is nested `o.M`: the `modifyId` copied from the corresponding modification request and pushed only for an `AMENDMENT` event whose request supplied it. The stream schema explicitly publishes `M` as a string even though modification requests use int64. The wrapper therefore preserves the exact wire value as nullable `string` rather than guessing a numeric normalization or losing identifiers beyond JavaScript's safe-integer range.

The complete event comparison exposed five additional gaps that the changelog alone did not mention. The shared event now retains the COIN-M top-level account alias `i` and nested margin asset `ma`, the USDⓈ-M ignored int64 strategy fields `si` and `ss`, and the product expiry-reason field `er`. The existing shared bid/ask values remain decimal but their public descriptions now state the product-specific meaning: notional for USDⓈ-M and base-asset quantity for COIN-M. Product-only fields are nullable so absence remains distinguishable and does not invent data for the other product.

The same full-payload test also proved that the model's existing wire enums and millisecond timestamps were not self-deserializing: mapped values such as `GTC` failed under the model's direct JSON contract, while transaction, order-trade, and GTD times lacked the converters already used by other Futures stream models. The affected event properties now apply the established map and date converters explicitly. Both existing user-data-stream clients already route this event into the same shared model, so no callback or transport expansion was needed.

Two deterministic no-network tests cover a complete USDⓈ-M amendment event, a COIN-M amendment with product fields, int64 precision, exact string `M`, conditional absence, product-only absence, enum mapping, and millisecond timestamp conversion. The complete suite now has 253 passing tests, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

## Backward Review 16: Futures order-modification lifecycle and forward query-risk review

The `c02c226..7245515` range was reviewed across Slices 71-75 rather than treating their task descriptions as proof of correctness. The current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M single and batch Modify Order, modification-history, WebSocket API `order.modify`, and `ORDER_TRADE_UPDATE` contracts were rechecked against the live canonical catalogs and schemas, the derivatives changelog effective 2026-07-30, and official generated JavaScript connector HEAD `092e4f2`; the connector's remote HEAD still matches that exact revision. Request paths, authentication, signed parameter placement, identifier precedence, mandatory post-migration quantity and price, int64 `modifyId`, product IP weights, receive-window behavior, per-item batch results, history shape, conditional string stream `M`, and product-only stream fields remain aligned. The published `price`/`priceMatch` contradictions also remain unresolved, so the existing fail-closed rejection is still safer than emitting a request prohibited by the same current contract.

No behavioral defect was found in the five reviewed slices. Two public documentation defects were closed. First, the WebSocket API options claimed that client-order-id adjustment defaults to false while the property currently defaults to true; the documentation now states the actual behavior without silently changing a public financial default during a review. Second, the same options now disclose the transport's real rate-limit boundary: Futures WebSocket query methods pass published `weight` values, but the shared request path does not consume them in a local limiter and discards the returned `rateLimits` counters after deserialization. The root client still applies server-directed 418/429 backoff, never automatically retries the failed operation, and enforces connection message limits. A limiter added only to the two reviewed mutation methods would therefore be false safety. A dedicated cross-method, multi-dimensional WebSocket API limiter decision remains necessary, including IP request weight, account order counters, response-counter reconciliation, and multi-client/process scope.

The forward review also proved that the 2026-08-05 All Orders work is broader and riskier than adding response properties. The shared order model already maps the announced `pair`, `cumBase`, `cumQuote`, and `goodTillDate` keys, but the executable contracts are stale: USDⓈ-M still accepts an absent symbol despite the current required-symbol contract; COIN-M lacks pair scope and its symbol-or-pair requirement, pair/order-id exclusion, maximum limit 100, seven-day window, and post-migration flat IP weight 5. Force Orders remains the next bounded group: both products omit the current limit parameter and query constraints/tests, while the announced cumulative/product fields require full response-schema verification and COIN-M documents 90-day retention. These silent request-contract gaps outrank proactive local WebSocket pacing because they can emit invalid signed queries or discard requested scope today, whereas the existing server guard prevents automatic retry escalation after a rate-limit response.

Sixteen targeted no-network tests cover all five reviewed slices; all 253 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target solution build succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. Diff whitespace, renamed batch-property call sites, public examples, model-field parity, and official connector freshness were also checked. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

2. Slice 77: fully align both User's Force Orders contracts, including limit, product constraints, weights, retention, complete response data, and deterministic tests.
3. Slice 78: inventory and decide the cross-method Futures WebSocket API multi-dimensional rate-limit architecture before changing transport behavior.

## Slice 76: current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M All Orders contracts

`GET /fapi/v1/allOrders` and `GET /dapi/v1/allOrders` were audited field by field against their live canonical Trade sections, the combined derivatives changelog effective 2026-08-05, and official generated JavaScript connector HEAD `092e4f2`. Both are signed USER_DATA query endpoints. USDⓈ-M requires a nonblank symbol, accepts an optional int64 order identifier, has server-default limit 500 and maximum 1000, and consumes IP weight 5. COIN-M requires exactly one nonblank symbol or pair; a pair query covers all symbols in that pair and cannot include an order identifier. Its server-default limit is 50, maximum is 100, and the post-CM-migration weight is flat IP weight 5 for either scope. The live COIN-M page still displays the obsolete 20-with-symbol/40-with-pair weights alongside the explicit post-migration replacement; the effective flat value is implemented rather than preserving the retired split.

For both products, an order identifier is an inclusive lower bound, an explicit query period must be shorter than seven days, and omitting the time bounds returns the recent seven days. The implementations now reject missing or ambiguous scope, a pair/order-id combination, non-forward or seven-day-and-longer explicit ranges, out-of-contract limits, and explicit or configured receive windows above 60,000 milliseconds before transport. All caller filters remain in the signed GET query. Retention is documented rather than guessed from the local clock: canceled or expired orders with no fill disappear after three days, and every order disappears after 90 days; Binance remains authoritative for whether a retained record is available.

The current shared response model already carried all keys announced on 2026-08-05, but that pre-existing shape did not make either operation complete. The full endpoint schemas were rechecked: both preserve decimal average price, cumulative quote and base values, executed and original quantity, prices, trigger/callback data, booleans, symbol and pair, int64 order identity, creation/update/GTD timestamps, side/status/position/time-in-force/order/working/price-match/self-trade-prevention enums, and the client order identifier. Public quantity descriptions no longer incorrectly assign cumulative quote and base values to only one Futures product. README and console examples now provide the newly enforced USDⓈ-M symbol.

Five deterministic no-network tests cover both signed GET routes, API-key authentication, exact flat weight, int64 query and response precision, symbol and pair scopes, all filters, current limits, strict seven-day boundary, receive-window configuration, complete shared response deserialization, and every local scope/identifier validation. All 258 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution build succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request was sent.

## Slice 77: current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M User's Force Orders contracts

`GET /fapi/v1/forceOrders` and `GET /dapi/v1/forceOrders` were audited independently against their live canonical Trade sections, the combined derivatives changelog effective 2026-08-05, and official generated JavaScript connector HEAD `092e4f2`. Both are signed USER_DATA queries with an optional symbol, optional `LIQUIDATION`/`ADL` filter, optional start and end times, server-default limit 50, maximum limit 100, and dynamic IP weight 20 with a symbol or 50 without one. The implementations already carried the correct dynamic weight but silently discarded the documented limit. They now serialize it, reject only values above the published maximum, reject a supplied blank symbol, expose the wire-accurate `autoCloseType` parameter name, and retain all filters in the signed GET query. The executable README and console examples now use symbol scope and the maximum limit, avoiding the heavier unscoped request in ordinary sample use.

The two products deliberately do not share an invented timing contract. USDⓈ-M states that omitting `startTime` queries the seven days before `endTime`, but publishes neither an explicit maximum range nor an endpoint-specific `recvWindow` maximum; its current generated connector agrees. The wrapper therefore preserves larger explicit/configured receive windows and does not copy a seven-day rejection from All Orders or another product. COIN-M explicitly caps `recvWindow` at 60,000 milliseconds and supports querying only the past 90 days. Explicit and configured COIN-M receive windows above that maximum now fail before transport. The 90-day statement is availability guidance rather than a specified maximum query span, so it is documented without a local-clock rejection; Binance remains authoritative for retained data. Neither current page publishes a minimum limit or a time-order validation rule, and the client does not invent either.

The current full response schemas share int64 order identity, symbol, pair, status, client order identifier, decimal price, average price, original/executed quantity, cumulative quote and base values, time-in-force, type, reduce/close booleans, side, position side, stop price, working/original type, and creation/update timestamps. COIN-M additionally publishes `priceProtect` and `goodTillDate`; USDⓈ-M does not. The shared order model already represented all of these keys, including the changelog's new USDⓈ-M `cumBase`/`pair` and COIN-M `cumQuote`/`goodTillDate`, so no endpoint-specific duplicate model was added.

Five deterministic no-network tests cover both signed paths, API-key authentication, all four symbol/unscoped weight outcomes, both close-type filters, every query field, maximum limit, blank-symbol and over-limit rejection, product-specific explicit/configured receive-window behavior, int64 precision, and complete USDⓈ-M and COIN-M response deserialization. ## Slice 78: Futures WebSocket API multi-dimensional rate-limit architecture decision

The complete executable Futures WebSocket API query surface was inventoried before changing shared transport behavior. USDⓈ-M has fourteen request paths across General, Market Data, Account, and Trade; COIN-M has nine across General, Account, and Trade. Every path enters one product-level `RequestAsync` method. Both transports accept one integer `weight`, but neither reads it. They have no field for the two order-count windows, deserialize successful `rateLimits` envelopes and return only the operation result, and do not reconcile counters from failed envelopes. Their implemented protections are ApiSharp's four-outgoing-messages-per-socket-per-second setting and the root client's server-directed 418/429 guard. That guard covers later WebSocket API requests in the same root client, does not replay the failed operation, and neither coordinates the separate REST client guard nor other roots or processes.

The live official contracts require more than a single weighted queue. USDⓈ-M defines `REQUEST_WEIGHT` per IP and `ORDERS` per UID, publishes 2,400 weight per minute plus 300 orders per ten seconds and 1,200 per minute, charges five weight for a handshake, and permits response counters to be hidden. It says the WebSocket IP pool is separate from ordinary REST weight except that REST single/batch order place, modify, and cancel operations enter the WebSocket pool; the UID order pools are shared with REST. The COIN-M WebSocket page instead says its limits are the same as REST and shared with REST. The effective 2026-06-30 UM/CM integration notice says UM and CM share one IP-weight pool and one account-order pool, but describes `fapi` and `dapi` counters without resolving how that statement combines with the USDⓈ-M WebSocket/REST exception. The pages therefore do not define one contradiction-free pool topology from which this library can safely infer cross-product and cross-transport keys.

Canonical sources: [USDⓈ-M WebSocket API General Info](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/products/derivatives-trading-usds-futures/websocket-api-general-info), [COIN-M WebSocket API General Info](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/products/derivatives-trading-coin-futures/websocket-api-general-info), and [COIN-M / USDⓈ-M Architecture Integration](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/products/derivatives-trading-coin-futures/Important-CM-UM-Integration-Notice).

The bounded decision is to install no proactive default limiter. A process-local queue would create false safety: the client cannot discover or uniquely key the caller's public IP or UID, API keys are not guaranteed to be UID identities, ad hoc requests can use another key on the same connection, handshake weight is outside the current request hook, REST overlap is owned by separate client instances, and multiple processes can consume the same exchange counters. The one `weight` integer also cannot represent simultaneous IP weight plus both UID order windows. Consuming returned counters without shared identities and an authoritative pool topology would only make an incomplete queue look informed.

A safe future implementation has explicit prerequisites rather than an implied follow-up coding slice: a Binance clarification of the post-integration WebSocket/REST pool topology; per-request metadata for IP weight and every order-count dimension; handshake accounting; success and error counter exposure/reconciliation; caller-supplied IP and UID scope identities; and an injectable coordinator capable of sharing state across REST, WebSocket, client instances, and processes. Building that system without those prerequisites would be overengineering and still fail the stated safety boundary. The current server-directed backoff remains active and its public limitation is now sharper: the per-connection message cap is not a weighted-quota or order-quota limiter. No production request or WebSocket connection was opened, and no runtime behavior changed.

All 263 deterministic tests still pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings.

### Revised next order

1. Perform mandatory Backward Review 17 across Slices 76-78, including documentation and execution-order review.
2. Re-rank the next bounded live-contract gap after Review 17; do not schedule a proactive Futures WebSocket limiter until its stated prerequisites are met.

## Backward Review 17: All Orders, Force Orders, and Futures WebSocket limiter decision

The complete `5b9a736..efc9175` range was reviewed across production clients, public interfaces, response models, deterministic tests, README and console examples, release notes, and the execution contract. The live USDⓈ-M and COIN-M All Orders and User's Force Orders sections, the combined derivatives changelog through its current 2026-08-07 head, both Futures WebSocket API general-limit pages, the effective UM/CM architecture-integration notice, and ApiSharp 4.5.1's public transport surface were rechecked. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

One public API safety defect was found in Slice 77. The newly added Force Orders `limit` occupied the old positional `receiveWindow` slot in both product methods. Because both values are `int?`, an existing source call could still compile while sending its receive window as a result limit or failing the new maximum-limit guard. That is a silent financial-request meaning change, not an acceptable consequence of moving to the current endpoint contract. The methods now keep `receiveWindow` in its established position and append `limit` after it; named calls remain unchanged and wire serialization still uses the current `limit` and `recvWindow` keys. A two-product regression test proves that the fifth positional argument remains `recvWindow` and does not emit `limit`.

The rest of Slices 76 and 77 remains aligned. USDⓈ-M All Orders requires symbol scope, while COIN-M requires exactly one symbol or pair and prohibits pair with order ID. The current limits, strict sub-seven-day explicit periods, 60,000-millisecond receive-window ceilings, post-migration flat weight 5, inclusive order-ID behavior, retention guidance, and complete product response fields remain correctly represented. Force Orders still uses weight 20 with symbol and 50 without symbol, accepts the current auto-close and time filters plus maximum limit 100, preserves USDⓈ-M's absence of an endpoint-specific receive-window maximum, applies COIN-M's explicit 60,000 maximum and 90-day availability guidance, and does not invent an undocumented minimum limit or maximum time span.

Slice 78's no-default-limiter decision also survives review. The USDⓈ-M page still separates WebSocket IP weight from ordinary REST weight except for named order mutations and shares UID order counters with REST; the COIN-M page still says its WebSocket limits are the same as and shared with REST; and the integration notice still merges UM/CM pools without resolving that cross-transport topology. The transport still has one unconsumed integer weight, no order-window metadata, no handshake accounting, discarded response counters, separate REST and WebSocket backoff guards, and no cross-root or cross-process identity. Installing a local proactive limiter would therefore still claim a safety boundary the library cannot enforce.

Forward review found a smaller current stream-contract gap ahead of broader Futures WebSocket authentication work. The official 2026-03-16 changelog says the USDⓈ-M per-symbol and all-market mark-price streams added wire field `ap` for mark-price moving average. Both wrapper subscriptions deserialize `BinanceFuturesUsdtStreamMarkPrice`, whose current model has no `ap` member, so the value is silently discarded. The legacy detail URLs currently redirect to Binance's generic landing page rather than rendering the stream schema; Slice 79 must therefore obtain the current canonical catalog schema and generated official connector evidence before choosing the property type or changing code. It is bounded to the two USDⓈ-M mark-price stream variants, their topics, update intervals, full payload, public links, and deterministic model/subscription tests. The separately announced aggregate-trade `nq` field remains an independent candidate and must not be pulled into Slice 79.

Sixteen targeted no-network cases cover both reviewed REST groups and transport backoff. All 264 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings.

### Revised next order

1. Slice 79: fully audit the USDⓈ-M per-symbol and all-market mark-price streams, including the missing `ap` field, against current canonical schema and generated-connector evidence.
2. Re-rank the separate aggregate-trade `nq` candidate and Futures WebSocket authentication gap after Slice 79; perform Backward Review 18 no later than the fourth post-review slice.

## Slice 79: current USDⓈ-M per-symbol and all-market mark-price streams

The two USDⓈ-M mark-price streams were compared field by field against the live canonical WebSocket Market Streams catalog, the 2026-03-16 USDⓈ-M changelog entry, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`. The per-symbol contract is `{symbol}@markPrice` at the default three-second cadence or `{symbol}@markPrice@1s` at the only explicit update speed. The all-market contract is `!markPrice@arr` or `!markPrice@arr@1s` with the same cadence rule. The wrapper's existing topic strings were already semantically correct: its explicit `3000` value selects the suffix-free default rather than manufacturing an undocumented `@3s` suffix. Topic creation is now deterministic and rejects null, empty, or blank symbol collections and unsupported intervals before any connection attempt. It deliberately permits CM names such as `BTCUSD_PERP`, which the merged stream accepts. The three public overloads point to the live canonical catalog anchors instead of legacy detail URLs that now redirect to Binance's generic landing page.

Both current response schemas contain event type/time, symbol, mark price, index price, estimated settlement price, funding rate, next funding time, the 2026-03-16 `ap` mark-price moving average, and post-UM/CM-integration integer `st` where 1 means UM and 2 means CM. The canonical schema and generated connector agree that `ap` is a numeric string and `st` is an integer, so `BinanceFuturesUsdtStreamMarkPrice` now exposes decimal `MarkPriceMovingAverage` and integer `SymbolType`. This is not just the changelog delta: `st` was also missing and was included because the endpoint was audited against its complete current schema. The all-market test deliberately exercises `st = 2`, because the current USDⓈ-M page says the merged stream can deliver CM symbols through `fstream`. COIN-M client/model changes remain outside this bounded USDⓈ-M slice and were not inferred from the USDⓈ-M page.

Three deterministic no-network tests cover canonical per-symbol and all-market topics, the suffix-free three-second default, the explicit one-second suffix, invalid symbol and interval rejection, complete per-symbol payload deserialization, merged CM-symbol delivery, decimal precision, both current symbol-type values, and millisecond timestamps. All 267 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance connection was opened.

### Revised next order

1. Slice 80: fully audit the USDⓈ-M aggregate-trade stream against its current schema, including `nq`, post-integration `st`, topic rules, and any fields retained locally but absent from the canonical payload.
2. Re-rank the Futures WebSocket authentication gap after Slice 80; perform mandatory Backward Review 18 no later than Slice 82.

## Slice 80: current USDⓈ-M aggregate-trade stream

The USDⓈ-M aggregate-trade stream was compared field by field against the live canonical WebSocket Market Streams catalog, the 2025-12-29 changelog change effective 2025-12-31, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`. The current stream is `{symbol}@aggTrade`, updates every 100 milliseconds, and aggregates market trades with the same price and taking side. Its complete payload is `e`, `E`, `s`, `a`, `p`, `q`, `nq`, `f`, `l`, `T`, `m`, and `st`. Decimal `q` includes all market-trade quantity, while decimal `nq` excludes trades involving RPI orders. Integer `st` identifies the post-integration product as 1 for UM or 2 for CM. The wrapper now exposes these last two missing fields as `NormalQuantity` and `SymbolType` without narrowing int64 aggregate and first/last trade identifiers.

The old USDⓈ-M overloads returned a shared Futures model that exposes boolean wire field `M` as `Ignore`. Neither the current USDⓈ-M canonical response, its example, nor the current USDⓈ-M generated connector contains that field. That shared model is also used by the COIN-M client, whose endpoint is outside this slice and was not changed without an independent audit. The USDⓈ-M overloads therefore now return product-specific `BinanceFuturesUsdtStreamAggregatedTrade`; it contains the complete current USDⓈ-M schema and no misleading default-false `Ignore` member, while COIN-M behavior remains untouched. Both public overloads point to the canonical catalog anchor. Topic creation shares the small symbol-topic helper introduced with Slice 79, rejects null, empty, or blank collections before transport, lowercases names, and does not invent a spot-style character whitelist that would reject derivative names such as `BTCUSD_PERP`.

Two new deterministic no-network tests cover exact topic creation, derivative-symbol preservation, missing-symbol rejection, complete current payload deserialization, decimal `q`/`nq` separation, int64 identifier precision, timestamps, `st = 2`, and removal of the stale wrapper-only property. Together with the Slice 79 stream regressions, all five targeted cases pass. All 269 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance connection was opened.

2. Slice 82: audit the equivalent COIN-M lifecycle independently, then perform mandatory Backward Review 18 before any further implementation chain.

## Slice 81: current USDⓈ-M WebSocket API user-data-stream lifecycle

The three USDⓈ-M WebSocket API user-data-stream operations were audited against the live canonical User Data Streams catalog, the current derivatives changelog, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`. All three use `wss://ws-fapi.binance.com/ws-fapi/v1`, consume IP weight 1, require an API key, and do not require a signature, timestamp, receive window, or caller-supplied listen key. Their exact method names are `userDataStream.start`, `userDataStream.ping`, and `userDataStream.stop`. The catalog request schemas and examples place `apiKey` inside `params`; the generated connector independently marks each request `WithAPIKey: true` and `Signed: false`. The wrapper now follows that API-key-only contract through its existing query transport rather than reusing a signed account method.

`userDataStream.start` returns the active `listenKey` and extends it for 60 minutes when the API key already owns a stream. `userDataStream.ping` extends that stream and returns the kept-alive `listenKey`; the 2024-04-19 changelog specifically records this response-field addition effective 2024-04-25. `userDataStream.stop` returns an empty result object, which the public method maps to success without inventing response data. All three response envelopes continue through the shared `id`, `status`, `result`, and `rateLimits` model. The public methods are grouped on a new `IBinanceFuturesSocketClientUsdQueryUserDataStream` surface as `StartUserDataStreamAsync`, `KeepAliveUserDataStreamAsync`, and `StopUserDataStreamAsync` so they are not confused with the retained REST `/fapi/v1/listenKey` lifecycle. The existing stream subscription accepts a listen key produced by either lifecycle.

The no-credential regression exposed an older USDⓈ-M WebSocket transport defect: reading `AuthenticationProvider` before proving credentials existed caused a `NullReferenceException` instead of the intended controlled error. The USDⓈ-M transport now tracks configured API-key and secret availability before provider creation. It performs time synchronization and signature generation only for signed calls; API-key-only calls add only `apiKey`. Existing signed USDⓈ-M account and trade requests preserve their prior timestamp/signature path. COIN-M remains outside this bounded slice and still requires its own audit.

Three deterministic no-network tests cover exact path, method, weight, API-key/signature metadata, public interface exposure, controlled missing-credential failures, both listen-key response shapes, the empty stop result, and returned request-weight counters. All 272 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request or connection was opened.

### Revised next order

1. Slice 82: audit and implement only the equivalent COIN-M WebSocket API `userDataStream.start`, `userDataStream.ping`, and `userDataStream.stop` lifecycle against its own live contract.
2. Perform mandatory Backward Review 18 across Slices 79-82 immediately after Slice 82; do not start another implementation slice before that review and re-ranking.

## Slice 82: current COIN-M WebSocket API user-data-stream lifecycle

The COIN-M lifecycle was audited independently against the live canonical COIN-M User Data Streams catalog and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`; the USDⓈ-M implementation was not treated as evidence. All three operations use `wss://ws-dapi.binance.com/ws-dapi/v1`, consume IP weight 1, require an API key, and do not require a signature, timestamp, receive window, or caller-supplied listen key. Their exact method names are `userDataStream.start`, `userDataStream.ping`, and `userDataStream.stop`. The catalog request schemas and examples put `apiKey` in `params`, while the generated connector independently marks all three requests `WithAPIKey: true`, `Signed: false`, and `WithSessionLogon: false`.

`userDataStream.start` returns the active `listenKey` and extends an existing stream for 60 minutes; `userDataStream.ping` extends it and returns the kept-alive `listenKey`; `userDataStream.stop` returns an empty result object. The current generated response models confirm both listen-key results and the empty close result. The catalog contains irrelevant generated descriptions such as `positionId`, combined-stream state, maximum orders, and 24-hour trade counts around these generic envelope fields; those prose fragments conflict with the actual examples and generated types and were not invented as response members. The combined derivatives changelog contains the 2024-04-19 `userDataStream.ping` response change only under USDⓈ-M and contains no equivalent COIN-M lifecycle entry, so that entry was deliberately not used as COIN-M proof.

The public lifecycle is exposed through new `IBinanceFuturesSocketClientCoinQueryUserDataStream` methods `StartUserDataStreamAsync`, `KeepAliveUserDataStreamAsync`, and `StopUserDataStreamAsync`. The existing REST `/dapi/v1/listenKey` lifecycle remains intact, and the COIN-M stream subscription now documents that either lifecycle can supply its listen key. The COIN-M query transport had the same pre-existing missing-credential `NullReferenceException` and unnecessary time synchronization for unsigned API-key-only calls found in Slice 81. It now checks configured API-key and secret availability before provider creation and performs time synchronization/signing only for signed calls; existing signed COIN-M account and trade requests preserve their timestamp/signature path.

Three deterministic no-network tests cover the exact path, methods, weight, API-key/signature metadata, interface exposure, controlled missing-key and missing-secret failures, both listen-key response shapes, the empty close result, and request-weight counters. All 275 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request or connection was opened.

2. Re-rank the remaining live-contract gaps during Review 18; do not begin another implementation slice before that evidence-backed decision.

## Backward Review 18: Futures market streams and WebSocket user-data lifecycles

The complete `efc9175..a03c47a` range was reviewed across production clients, public interfaces and models, deterministic tests, README and console examples, release notes, and the execution contract. This range deliberately includes the Review 17 follow-up commit `57ebbd6`, because it contains the public positional-safety correction made immediately before Slice 79. The live USDⓈ-M market-stream catalog, both live Futures WebSocket API User Data Streams catalogs, the current derivatives integration notice, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1` were rechecked on 2026-08-09. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

The Review 17 Force Orders correction remains sound: the pre-existing fifth positional argument is still `receiveWindow`, the new `limit` remains appended after it, named calls are unchanged, and the wire request still emits the current `limit` and `recvWindow` keys. Slice 79 also remains aligned. The USDⓈ-M per-symbol and all-market mark-price topics retain the suffix-free three-second default and explicit `@1s` form, reject unsupported intervals and invalid symbol collections before transport, and deserialize the complete current `e/E/s/p/i/P/r/ap/T/st` payload. Slice 80 retains its product-specific USDⓈ-M aggregate-trade model, complete `e/E/s/a/p/q/nq/f/l/T/m/st` payload, int64 identifiers, and removal of the absent `M` field without changing the independently owned COIN-M contract.

Slices 81 and 82 remain symmetric where their independently verified contracts agree. USDⓈ-M uses `wss://ws-fapi.binance.com/ws-fapi/v1`; COIN-M uses `wss://ws-dapi.binance.com/ws-dapi/v1`. Both expose `userDataStream.start`, `userDataStream.ping`, and `userDataStream.stop` as API-key-only, unsigned, weight-1 operations with no timestamp, receive window, signature, or caller-supplied listen key. Start and ping return `listenKey`; stop maps the documented empty result to success. Credential guards run before authentication-provider access, API-key-only calls do not trigger time synchronization, and existing signed account/trade calls retain the secret/timestamp/signature path. Root-level credential replacement reaches both product clients through explicit concrete dispatch. The successful envelope's counters are still parsed internally but the public lifecycle returns only its operation result; that is consistent with Slice 78's reviewed decision not to invent an incomplete proactive limiter or counter coordinator.

No new production defect was found in Slices 79-82, so Review 18 makes no code correction. Seventeen targeted no-network regressions cover Force Orders positional semantics, both USDⓈ-M stream groups, and both WebSocket user-data lifecycles. All 275 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. Repository diff checks report no whitespace errors.

Forward review found a smaller verified data-loss gap ahead of the broader Futures WebSocket session-authentication work. The effective UM/CM integration notice explicitly adds integer `st` (`1` = UM, `2` = CM) to COIN-M `<symbol>@aggTrade`, and the current generated COIN-M response model contains `e`, `E`, `a`, `s`, `p`, `q`, `f`, `l`, `T`, `m`, and `st`. The wrapper's COIN-M subscriptions still return shared `BinanceFuturesStreamAggregatedTrade`, which silently drops `st` and exposes old boolean wire field `M` as `Ignore`, although neither the current generated model nor its serialized field set contains `M`. Unlike USDⓈ-M, the current COIN-M response has no `nq`; Slice 83 must not infer that field across products. The same integration notice also proves `st` changes in the COIN-M mark-price family and several other streams, but those contracts must remain separate bounded audits rather than one migration-wide rewrite.

## Slice 84: current COIN-M mark-price stream family

The COIN-M per-symbol and all-symbols-of-pair mark-price streams were compared field by field against the live canonical WebSocket Streams catalog, the COIN-M changelog's 2026-06-10 architecture-integration entry effective 2026-06-30, the linked UM/CM integration notice, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`. Both cataloged stream variants use `wss://dstream.binance.com`, default to the suffix-free 3000-millisecond topic, accept only the explicit `@1s` speed suffix, and expose the complete `e/E/s/p/P/i/r/T/st` payload. Delivery contracts represent funding rate as an empty string and next funding time as zero; the wrapper maps those sentinels to nullable `FundingRate = null` and `NextFundingTime = default(DateTime)`.

The integration notice independently defines `!markPrice@arr` and `!markPrice@arr@1s`, allows the mark-price family on both `fstream` and `dstream`, and requires integer `st` (`1` = UM, `2` = CM) on each payload. The live COIN-M catalog still has no all-market mark-price page, and the notice explicitly says standalone CM pages will be added in a future release. That absence does not leave the payload unknowable: the live USDⓈ-M all-market catalog page explicitly says the same stream supports both hosts and either product's symbols, and defines the complete `e/E/s/p/i/P/r/ap/T/st` response. The COIN-M all-market subscription and its public XML therefore use this cross-host canonical contract, including decimal mark-price moving average `ap`, rather than stopping at the narrower notice delta.

`BinanceFuturesCoinStreamMarkPrice` now exposes `SymbolType` and uses inherited `IndexPrice` and `EstimatedSettlePrice` properties instead of hiding the latter with a second `P`-mapped property. This removes a base/derived value split without changing wire names. The cross-host array now returns product-neutral `BinanceFuturesStreamAllMarketMarkPrice` from both USDⓈ-M and COIN-M clients; this intentional public callback-type change exposes `ap` without falsely adding that absent field to the cataloged COIN-M per-symbol and pair models. Per-symbol topic generation now rejects null, empty, or blank collections; pair generation rejects null or blank values and lowercases valid pairs. Per-symbol, pair, and all-market helpers all reject unsupported intervals before transport and emit the exact suffix-free 3000-millisecond or `@1s` forms. Public XML links, README, console examples, and release notes cover all three variants.

Four new deterministic regressions cover COIN-M topic formation/validation, the complete per-symbol payload, the complete delivery-contract pair payload and its funding sentinels, and the complete cross-host all-market payload including `ap` and `st`. Existing USDⓈ-M all-market coverage also uses the product-neutral model. All seven mark-price tests pass, all 281 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No live Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

## Slice 86: current merged contract-info stream

The contract-info stream was compared field by field against the live canonical COIN-M and USDⓈ-M WebSocket Streams catalogs, the COIN-M changelog's 2026-06-10 architecture-integration entry effective 2026-06-30, the linked integration notice, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`. Pair `ps` is present for COIN-M data but absent from the current USDⓈ-M schema, while bracket array `bks` appears only when bracket data changes. Integer `st` identifies UM as 1 and CM as 2.

The shared `BinanceFuturesStreamSymbolUpdate` model now exposes `SymbolType`, retains nullable brackets, and correctly converts mapped contract-type and symbol-status wire strings. The shared enum surface now matches the current union published by both products: the malformed `NEXT_QUARTER DELIVERING` map was corrected, the undocumented generic `DELIVERING` contract type and six stale non-Futures statuses were removed, and `DELIVERED`, `TRADING_HALT`, and `TRADING_CANCEL_ONLY` were added. Maintenance ratio `mmr` remains `decimal`, preserving more financial precision than the documented floating-point wire number. Both product clients retain the exact topic through explicit tested constants, and their public XML links now target the canonical endpoint pages. README, console examples, and release notes expose the COIN-M subscription.

## Backward Review 19: merged Futures market-stream contracts and USDⓈ-M channel routing

The complete `ba8f908..d64e944` range was reviewed across production clients, public interfaces and models, deterministic tests, README and console examples, release notes, and the execution contract. The four reviewed payload families remain aligned with the current official contracts. This is not a compatibility promise for those paths. Twenty targeted regressions across Slices 83-86 and the routing correction pass, all 290 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. ## Slice 87: current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M liquidation stream family

The USDⓈ-M and COIN-M individual-symbol and all-market liquidation streams were compared field by field against both live canonical WebSocket Stream catalogs, the COIN-M changelog's 2026-06-10 architecture-integration entry effective 2026-06-30, the linked UM/CM integration notice, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`. The individual topic is `{symbol}@forceOrder`, the all-market topic is `!forceOrder@arr`, and both publish at most one snapshot per symbol per 1000 milliseconds. USDⓈ-M uses the current Market channel fixed in Review 19; COIN-M uses `wss://dstream.binance.com/stream`. The 2026-04-10 changelog says the USDⓈ-M description changed from the latest to the largest liquidation within the interval, while both current live catalogs again say latest. Neither payload nor generated connector exposes a client-side discriminator, so the wrapper documents a neutral liquidation-order snapshot and does not invent selection logic.

The current wire shape is not uniform enough to flatten safely. USDⓈ-M individual events contain outer `e/E` and inner order `o`; COIN-M individual events additionally carry pair `ps` inside that order. On merged all-market events, `st` is always outside the order and identifies UM as 1 or CM as 2. UM events carry pair `ps` outside the order, while CM events retain `ps` inside the order. This product-dependent location is confirmed independently by both live examples and both generated connector model pairs. The COIN-M all-market catalog example incorrectly labels a CM symbol with `st: 1`; the effective integration notice explicitly defines CM as 2, so the implementation and regression use 2 rather than copying the contradictory example value.

`BinanceFuturesStreamLiquidation` now represents the complete outer event instead of the nested order. Its `Order` contains symbol, optional COIN-M pair, mapped side/order-type/time-in-force/status values, decimal quantities and prices, and trade time; the outer event exposes optional UM pair and merged symbol type. The obsolete extra `BinanceFuturesStreamLiquidationData` wrapper was removed. All six public subscription callbacks now return the complete event rather than silently unwrapping and losing event time, pair, and symbol type. This is an intentional public model-shape correction under the current-contract-only policy. Topic helpers reject null, empty, or blank symbol collections before transport, preserve dated COIN-M symbols, and lock both exact topic forms. Canonical XML links and executable README/console examples cover both products.

Five deterministic regressions cover both product topic builders and validation, complete USDⓈ-M and COIN-M individual payloads, the outer UM pair location, the inner CM pair location, mapped enums, decimal precision, timestamps, and both all-market symbol types. All five targeted tests and all 295 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

## Slice 88: current standard USDⓈ-M and COIN-M partial/diff depth stream family

The four standard depth contracts were compared field by field against the live USDⓈ-M Public and COIN-M WebSocket Stream catalogs, the effective UM/CM architecture-integration notice, the COIN-M changelog entry that links that notice, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`. Partial depth uses `{symbol}@depth{levels}@{updateSpeed}` with levels 5, 10, or 20; diff depth uses `{symbol}@depth@{updateSpeed}`. Both products accept only explicit `100ms` and `500ms` speed values. Omitting the optional speed suffix selects the documented 250-millisecond default. The catalog prose lists 250 milliseconds among the resulting publication cadences, but both live parameter enums and generated connector speed enums exclude explicit `250ms`; the wrapper therefore no longer generates the invalid `@250ms` topic.

The current shared payload is `e/E/T/s/ps/U/u/pu/b/a/st` for both partial and diff updates. Pair `ps` and integer symbol type `st` are present after the architecture integration, with `st` equal to 1 for USDⓈ-M and 2 for COIN-M. The integration notice names partial depth in its `ps/st` lists but omits diff depth, while both current endpoint catalogs and all four generated connector response models include the fields on diff depth too. The endpoint-specific current contracts are used rather than preserving that incomplete delta list. Both current COIN-M depth examples incorrectly label a COIN-M symbol with `st: 1`; deterministic coverage follows the notice's explicit semantic definition and uses 2.

`BinanceFuturesStreamOrderBookDepth` now preserves pair and symbol type, keeps all sequence identifiers as int64, and treats the documented first update ID as a non-nullable value. Partial callbacks no longer overwrite the payload symbol by parsing the subscription topic. Exact topic helpers reject null, empty, or blank symbol collections, unsupported levels, and unsupported explicit speeds before transport while preserving dated COIN-M symbols. Canonical XML links describe the suffix-free default correctly, README/console examples no longer send `@250ms`, and the previously absent COIN-M partial/diff subscription examples were added. Standard depth remains explicitly separate from RPI depth, whose topic includes RPI orders and has a different fixed cadence and publication surface.

Four deterministic regressions cover every partial/diff topic form for both products, all supported levels and explicit speeds, suffix-free defaults, invalid input rejection, complete USDⓈ-M and COIN-M payloads, int64 sequence boundaries, decimal price/quantity levels, timestamps, pair, and both symbol types. All four targeted tests and all 299 deterministic tests pass. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

## Slice 89: current USDⓈ-M RPI diff-depth stream

The current RPI stream was compared against the live USDⓈ-M Public WebSocket Stream catalog, the USDⓈ-M changelog entry dated 2025-11-25, the effective UM/CM architecture-integration notice, the complete live COIN-M stream catalog, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`. The changelog introduced both `GET /fapi/v1/rpiDepth` and `<symbol>@rpiDepth@500ms` only for USDⓈ-M. The current stream has one fixed topic, `{symbol}@rpiDepth@500ms`, runs on `wss://fstream.binance.com/public/stream`, and pushes every 500 milliseconds. Unlike standard depth, it includes and aggregates RPI orders.

Its complete current payload is `e/E/T/s/U/u/pu/b/a/ps/st`, identical in shape to the corrected shared standard-depth model from Slice 88. Pair `ps` and symbol type `st` were added by the architecture integration; current USDⓈ-M events use `st = 1`. Reusing `BinanceFuturesStreamOrderBookDepth` therefore preserves the contract without introducing a duplicate model. The catalog also defines a critical zero-quantity ambiguity: zero can mean all quotations at the price were filled or canceled, or that crossed RPI quantity is hidden. Both public overloads document this rather than allowing consumers to assume an unambiguous empty level.

The integration notice lists `<symbol>@rpiDepth` among streams receiving `st` and the UM `@500ms` topic among streams receiving `ps`, but it does not declare cross-host subscription for this family. The current COIN-M catalog contains no RPI depth endpoint, and the generated connector contains only the USDⓈ-M RPI stream model and operation. The wrapper consequently exposes only USDⓈ-M single-symbol and multi-symbol subscriptions and does not infer a COIN-M method from integration-delta wording.

The two overloads use the canonical Public transport path, exact fixed-speed topics, and the complete shared depth callback. The topic helper rejects null, empty, or blank symbol collections before transport. README and executable console examples expose pair and symbol type without adding a caller-selectable cadence that the endpoint does not support. Two deterministic regressions cover exact multi-symbol topics, invalid symbol collections, the complete current payload, int64 sequence boundaries, decimal price/quantity levels, timestamps, pair, symbol type, and a zero-quantity update. Both targeted tests and all 301 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

## Slice 90: current individual and merged Futures mini-ticker streams

The USDⓈ-M and COIN-M individual-symbol and all-market mini-ticker contracts were compared field by field against both live canonical WebSocket Stream catalogs, the 2026-06-10 architecture-integration changelog entry effective 2026-06-30, the linked integration notice, and official generated Go connector HEAD `a0c61d1`. Individual streams use `{symbol}@miniTicker`; USDⓈ-M publishes every two seconds on the Market channel and COIN-M every 500 milliseconds on `dstream`. The all-market topic is `!miniTicker@arr`, publishes changed symbols every 1000 milliseconds, and now returns the same merged UM + CM universe from either host.

The complete current event shape is `e/E/s/ps/c/o/h/l/v/q/st` for both products and both subscription forms. Pair `ps` and integer symbol type `st` are now present, with `st = 1` for USDⓈ-M and `st = 2` for COIN-M. The current COIN-M individual and all-market examples incorrectly label COIN-M symbols with `st: 1`; the effective integration notice explicitly defines CM as 2, so the implementation and tests use 2 rather than copying those contradictory example values.

The wire shape is shared but its volume semantics are not. For USDⓈ-M, `v` is base-asset volume and `q` is quote-asset volume. For COIN-M, `v` is total contract volume and `q` is base-asset volume. Both previous all-market callbacks therefore mislabeled counter-product events, and the old COIN-M model also exposed its `v` contract count as `QuoteVolume`. A single `BinanceFuturesStreamMiniTick` now preserves the raw `v` and `q` values, pair, symbol type, and all prices, then exposes nullable product-safe `BaseAssetVolume`, `QuoteAssetVolume`, and `ContractVolume` projections selected by `st`. Both clients use this shared model for individual and merged callbacks. The misleading `BinanceFuturesStreamCoinMiniTick` type was removed rather than retained as a stale historical contract; this is an intentional public breaking correction under the current-contract-only policy.

Exact topic helpers now reject null, empty, or blank symbol collections while preserving perpetual and dated COIN-M symbols. Both all-market subscriptions retain exact `!miniTicker@arr`; USDⓈ-M stays on the canonical Market channel. COIN-M public XML links now target the live catalog instead of retired detail pages, and README/console examples demonstrate the product-safe volume properties for both individual and merged events.

Five deterministic regressions cover both products' topic formation and validation, complete USDⓈ-M and COIN-M individual payloads, decimal precision, event timestamps, pair, symbol type, and a merged array that proves the same raw fields resolve to different safe volume properties. All five targeted tests and all 306 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

The individual and merged mini-ticker models exactly retain `e/E/s/ps/c/o/h/l/v/q/st`; the full-ticker models exactly retain `e/E/s/ps/p/P/w/c/Q/o/h/l/v/q/O/C/F/L/n/st`. Both families preserve raw `v/q`, resolve product-safe volume projections through `st`, keep USDⓈ-M on the Market channel and COIN-M on `dstream`, and use the documented individual and all-market cadences. The COIN-M pages still contradict the integration notice by showing `st: 1` in examples; the implementation correctly follows the notice's explicit `1 = UM` and `2 = CM` definition. The eight corresponding generated connector models have the same JSON-tag sets as the local models. Repository-wide scans found no stale host-specific ticker type or unsupported public raw-trade call site, while the supported aggregate-trade streams and private `ORDER_TRADE_UPDATE` flow remain intact.

Forward review found a pre-existing, higher-priority transport defect outside the reviewed range. The USDⓈ-M listen-key subscription still delegates to the legacy unrouted `/stream` address. Binance's current connection notice says that legacy routes were decommissioned on 2026-04-23 and that private streams must use the routed `/private` endpoint; an unrouted Market or Private connection no longer pushes data. This subscription therefore cannot be treated as production-safe. Slice 93 must audit and implement the complete current private connection grammar, including listen-key and event query parameters, envelope handling, and reconnect behavior. COIN-M `dstream` must remain outside that change unless its own current documentation supplies equivalent evidence.

The next financial-data risk is the kline family. Current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M schemas define product-dependent `v/q/V/Q` meanings and an int64 trade count, while the local COIN-M property names flatten contract and base-asset volumes and both models retain an `int` counter. Continuous-contract callbacks additionally discard outer `ps/ct`; the COIN-M callback uses the USDⓈ-M inner model, and the nested payload has no symbol field to recover the lost pair. These are separate public-contract decisions and must not be bundled with private routing.

Sixteen targeted mini-ticker, full-ticker, aggregate-trade, public-surface, and socket-address regressions pass, all 312 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings.

## Slice 93: current USDⓈ-M private listen-key stream route

The live USDⓈ-M User Data Streams connection section, every current event section, the important WebSocket route-migration notice, the effective 2026-04-23 legacy-route decommissioning entry, and official generated Python connector HEAD `d9be6628` were compared against the complete local listen-key subscription path. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

The old subscription connected to the unrouted `wss://fstream.binance.com/stream` address. Binance now states that unrouted Market and Private connections no longer push data. The subscription therefore exposed a valid-looking `WebSocketUpdateSubscription` flow whose account, position, and order events could silently never arrive. It now connects to `wss://fstream.binance.com/private/stream` and sends the existing JSON `SUBSCRIBE` request with the case-sensitive listen key as its sole topic. This matches the current generated connector's private-channel subscription mode. The migration notice also documents URL-query `listenKey/events` filtering and direct `/private/ws/<listenKey>` access, but neither is mandatory for an unfiltered subscription; adding a new filter surface or switching envelope mode would be unnecessary public churn.

The current user-data page lists `MARGIN_CALL`, `ACCOUNT_UPDATE`, `ORDER_TRADE_UPDATE`, `ACCOUNT_CONFIG_UPDATE`, `TRADE_LITE`, `CONDITIONAL_ORDER_TRIGGER_REJECT`, `STRATEGY_UPDATE`, deprecated-but-currently-documented `GRID_UPDATE`, `ALGO_UPDATE`, and `listenKeyExpired`. The local dispatcher already covers all ten event names and consumes the combined `stream/data` envelope, so no event callback or model was changed. ApiSharp 4.5.1 retains the subscription request on the connection and resends that same request after reconnect; moving the connection address therefore preserves listen-key resubscription behavior on the private channel. Blank listen keys are now rejected before a connection attempt, while nonblank keys retain exact casing and content.

README and console samples require no call-site change because the public method signature and callback set remain current. Public XML documentation now explicitly identifies the routed private channel and links both the current user-data page and migration notice. The release notes record the route correction and blank-key guard.

Three deterministic socket-address/topic tests prove the Market, Public, and Private addresses, exact listen-key preservation, and null/empty/blank rejection. All 314 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings.

### Revised next order

1. Slice 94: audit the individual USDⓈ-M and COIN-M `<symbol>@kline_<interval>` family, including product-safe volume semantics, int64 counters, exact topics, models, examples, and deterministic tests.
2. Slice 95: audit continuous-contract kline streams separately, retaining outer pair/contract identity and product-specific inner values.
3. Re-rank index-price and mark-price kline candidates after Slice 95.
4. Perform Backward Review 22 immediately after Slice 96 at the latest and before any Slice 97 implementation.

## Slice 94: current standard USDⓈ-M and COIN-M kline streams

The individual-symbol `<symbol>@kline_<interval>` operations were compared field by field against the live USDⓈ-M Market and COIN-M WebSocket Stream catalogs, the effective UM/CM integration notice, the current routed-connection rules, and official generated Python connector HEAD `d9be6628`. Both products use the exact lowercase topic, publish every 250 milliseconds when a current kline exists, and support `1m/3m/5m/15m/30m/1h/2h/4h/6h/8h/12h/1d/3d/1w/1M`; neither current stream supports `1s`. USDⓈ-M remains on the routed Market channel and COIN-M remains on `dstream`. No production Binance request or WebSocket connection was opened.

The complete event is outer `e/E/s/k` plus nested `t/T/s/i/f/L/o/c/h/l/v/n/x/q/V/Q/B`. The previous callbacks discarded outer event type and event time by returning only `k`; both product models now inherit the Futures event contract and the handlers copy current outer `e/E` values onto the returned kline. The documented ignore field `B` is retained, and `n` is now int64 rather than overflowing above `int.MaxValue`.

Volume meanings are product-dependent. USDⓈ-M defines `v/q/V/Q` as base-asset volume, quote-asset volume, taker-buy base-asset volume, and taker-buy quote-asset volume. COIN-M defines them as contract volume, base-asset volume, taker-buy contract volume, and taker-buy base-asset volume. The public properties now state those exact units; the misleading historical `Volume`, `QuoteVolume`, `TakerBuyBaseVolume`, and `TakerBuyQuoteVolume` names were removed rather than retained as aliases. This is an intentional public breaking correction.

The integration notice says either `fstream` or `dstream` can subscribe to either product after migration, but this event contains no `st` product discriminator. The wrapper therefore cannot safely infer volume units from the payload. Public documentation explicitly requires callers to use the USDⓈ-M callback/model for USDⓈ-M symbols and the COIN-M callback/model for COIN-M symbols even though Binance transport can carry either. Inventing symbol-pattern inference would be unsafe.

The USDⓈ-M public overloads also exposed a `premiumIndex` boolean that generated an undocumented `p<SYMBOL>@kline_<interval>` stream. Current catalogs and generated connectors contain no premium-index WebSocket stream; premium-index kline remains a REST operation. The switch and generated topic were removed under the current-contract-only policy. Shared topic validation now rejects null, empty, or blank symbol/interval collections, unsupported and one-second intervals, and more than the documented 1024 streams per connection. README and console examples demonstrate product-specific volume units and now include the four standard COIN-M overloads.

Five deterministic regressions cover both products' exact topics and validation, dated COIN-M symbols, complete combined payloads, outer event propagation, decimal precision, timestamps, near-int64-limit trade IDs, above-int32 trade counts, interval conversion, product-specific volumes, ignore fields, stale-property absence, and removal of the undocumented premium switch. All 319 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three known warnings.

### Revised next order

1. Slice 95: audit USDⓈ-M and COIN-M continuous-contract kline streams, preserving outer `ps/ct`, nested event data, and product-specific volumes without inheriting standard-symbol assumptions.
2. Re-rank COIN-M index-price and mark-price kline streams after Slice 95.
3. Perform Backward Review 22 immediately after Slice 96 at the latest and before any Slice 97 implementation.

## Slice 95: current USDⓈ-M and COIN-M continuous-contract kline streams

The `<pair>_<contractType>@continuousKline_<interval>` family was compared against the live routed USDⓈ-M Market and COIN-M WebSocket Stream catalogs, the effective UM/CM integration notice, and official generated Python connector HEAD `d9be6628`. Because the generated schemas omit nested symbol identity and the current official COIN-M cadence text conflicts with generated 250-millisecond metadata, unauthenticated read-only production subscriptions were also opened for `btcusdt_perpetual@continuousKline_1m` and `btcusd_perpetual@continuousKline_1m`, capturing at least one market payload from each. They performed no account, order, or mutation operation and were closed after observation.

Both live payloads confirmed outer `e/E/ps/ct/k` and nested `t/T/i/f/L/o/c/h/l/v/n/x/q/V/Q/B`; nested `s` is absent. The previous callbacks returned only `k`, losing event type, event time, pair, and continuous contract type, while the COIN-M callback incorrectly deserialized the nested data through the USDⓈ-M standard-kline wrapper. Each product now exposes a dedicated continuous-kline outer model and dedicated nested model. Pair and contract type remain in their actual outer locations, `ct` uses current wire mapping, and the nested IDs and trade count are int64. Stale standard-symbol assumptions are not present on either nested model.

Volume meaning remains product-dependent and was corroborated by the live values. USDⓈ-M uses base-asset volume, quote-asset volume, taker-buy base-asset volume, and taker-buy quote-asset volume for `v/q/V/Q`. COIN-M uses contract volume, base-asset volume, taker-buy contract volume, and taker-buy base-asset volume. The COIN-M callback can no longer silently expose USDⓈ-M volume property names.

The current operation enums also differ. USDⓈ-M supports `perpetual`, `current_quarter`, `next_quarter`, and `tradifi_perpetual`, plus intervals `1s/1m/3m/5m/15m/30m/1h/2h/4h/6h/8h/12h/1d/3d/1w/1M`. COIN-M supports only the first three non-TradFi contract types and the same interval set without `1s`. `TRADIFI_PERPETUAL` was added to the shared contract-type enum because the current USDⓈ-M operation requires it; endpoint-specific validation rejects unrelated shared enum values, rejects COIN-M `1s` and TradFi requests, validates pairs, and enforces the documented 1024-stream connection ceiling. Topics retain exact lowercase pair and contract tokens while the `continuousKline` segment remains case-sensitive.

After migration, either host may transport either product, but this payload has no `st` product discriminator. The wrapper cannot infer whether `v/q/V/Q` use USDⓈ-M or COIN-M units, so public XML explicitly requires the matching product client and callback model. No symbol-pattern inference or silent cross-product conversion was added.

The COIN-M catalog says “Kline update every second” while both current generated stream methods label update speed as 250 milliseconds. The brief live observation confirmed the shape but did not establish a stable cadence. This official-source contradiction is recorded rather than converted into a false API guarantee; the operation has no cadence parameter, so it does not alter subscription construction or payload handling.

Five new deterministic regressions cover exact topics, the USDⓈ-M-only one-second and TradFi contracts, COIN-M rejections, null/empty/blank/undefined/limit guards, complete combined payloads, pair and contract identity, timestamps, decimal precision, near-int64-limit update IDs, above-int32 trade counts, interval and contract conversion, product-specific volume units, ignore fields, and public callback types. README and console examples now show outer identity and product-specific nested volumes and include both COIN-M overloads. Ten targeted continuous/enum tests and all 324 deterministic tests pass.
