## Slice 119: COIN-M history-download limiter and contract alignment

All six current COIN-M Account history-download pages were re-read on 2026-08-10 from the rendered official catalog. The three ID operations remain signed `GET /dapi/v1/income/asyn`, `GET /dapi/v1/order/asyn`, and `GET /dapi/v1/trade/asyn`; each consumes IP weight 1000, permits at most two calls per minute before the third call triggers a ban, shares an eight-download monthly limit with the website download page, and accepts a requested interval no longer than one year. The wrapper previously assigned weight 5 to all three, which could undercount one request by 995 and make a generic limiter admit a ban-triggering third call. All three now report weight 1000 to the configured limiter.

The corresponding signed link operations remain `GET /dapi/v1/income/asyn/id`, `GET /dapi/v1/order/asyn/id`, and `GET /dapi/v1/trade/asyn/id`, all at IP weight 5. The order and trade link weights were already current; transaction history incorrectly used weight 20 and is now weight 5. All three reject null, empty, or whitespace download identifiers before signing or transport. The six public methods now expose the published int64 `recvWindow`, normalize configured defaults without narrowing to int32, and reject values over 60,000 ms before any request.

The one-year statement is preserved exactly as published in the public XML contract. Binance does not define whether its boundary means a fixed 365-day duration or a calendar-year calculation, so the client does not invent a potentially incompatible leap-year preflight rule. Likewise, the shared eight-per-month allowance includes website activity and cannot be truthfully enforced by process-local state. The exact request weight and official operational warnings are therefore exposed without a false local guarantee.

Endpoint-level response reconciliation found one additional field omitted by the inventory: current link responses publish `notified`, now represented on `BinanceFuturesDownloadLink`. The current schema is internally inconsistent for `isExpired`: its description is boolean, but the rendered type and generated official connector declare string and publish `"null"`. Replacing the semantic nullable boolean with an arbitrary string would make the public model worse. A bounded converter now accepts JSON boolean, null, and the published string boolean/`"null"` forms while retaining `bool?` for consumers; unexpected values still fail deserialization instead of being silently coerced.

All six retired Account URLs now use their exact current COIN-M catalog anchors, and the retired COIN-M hierarchy residue falls from 61 to 55 links. Eleven focused Coin Account tests pass, including all six paths, signed parameters, exact 1000/5 weights, current response fields, the published string-null expiry sample, blank identifier rejection, and explicit/configured receive-window ceilings. The complete deterministic suite passes 436/436 tests. A forced full multi-target rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three pre-existing warnings. No live Binance request, authenticated account query, download allocation, WebSocket connection, order, cancellation, listen-key mutation, or other mutation was sent.

Review 27 is now mandatory before further implementation: Slices 114-116 and 119 are the four development turns since Review 26. After that review, the planned implementation order remains Slices 120-124, Review 28, Slices 125-128, Slice 117, Review 29, and Slice 118. The measurable remaining minimum is fourteen small turns and approximately 12-36 active hours. There are 117 completed development slices out of the currently numbered 128; this approximately 91.4% planning ratio still excludes Options-discovered implementation work and the actual FIX/SBE implementation.

## Backward Review 27: Slices 114-116 and 119

Review 27 examined the complete `308fc27..4b1afc4` range rather than trusting the four slice summaries. The review covered the shared Spot/USDⓈ-M WebSocket session-transition gate, both session authentication implementations and tests, the complete USDⓈ-M WebSocket API Market Data correction, the COIN-M closing inventory and execution queue, all six COIN-M history-download calls, their response converter, release notes, this execution contract, and the external tracking plan.

The review found one real concurrency defect in Slice 114. Its deterministic gate test correctly required the reconnect authentication decision to be evaluated only after a preceding logout completed, but the production `AuthenticateSessionAsync` methods did not re-read `LifecycleSubscription.Authenticated` after acquiring the gate. If reconnect authentication was queued while logout was still running, logout could set the lifecycle to unauthenticated and the queued reconnect would nevertheless send `session.logon`. The same stale decision could re-enable USDⓈ-M authentication after an API-key revocation disabled automatic re-authentication. Both Spot and USDⓈ-M reconnect paths now re-check the connection-scoped authentication requirement inside the serialized transition and return successful connection restoration without sending logon when logout or revocation has won the ordering.

The gate itself remains appropriately bounded: it serializes only rare session lifecycle transitions, converts cancellation while waiting into the library's cancellation result, and always releases after transition completion or failure. The focused review suite now also proves that Spot logout and USDⓈ-M revocation clear the exact state consumed by the reconnect decision. No broader WebSocket request serialization was introduced.

Slice 115 required no correction. Its depth default and discrete 2/5/10/20 weights, exact accepted limits, required-symbol guards, WebSocket-specific int64 book-ticker update ID, response time/price preservation, and RPI documentation remain internally consistent. Slice 116 also remains a truthful inventory: the 64 rendered REST routes plus three effective integration-notice Algo routes are still separated from implementation, and the ten remediation groups remain small risk-owned units rather than one mutation-heavy batch.

Slice 119 required no additional endpoint correction. The three download-ID calls report weight 1000, all three link calls report weight 5, all six use signed int64 receive windows capped at 60,000 ms, blank identifiers fail before transport, the shared response exposes `notified`, and expiry parsing is strict outside the documented boolean/null/string variants. Its six owned current links reduce the remaining retired COIN-M hierarchy residue to 55. The deliberate absence of a guessed 365-day/calendar-year guard and a false process-local monthly counter remains correct because the official one-year boundary is undefined and the monthly allowance is shared with website activity.

Sixteen focused session/gate regressions and the complete 437-test deterministic suite pass. A forced full multi-target rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three pre-existing warnings. No live Binance request, authenticated query, WebSocket connection, session logon/logout, download allocation, order, cancellation, listen-key mutation, or other mutation was sent.

The forward contract is unchanged except for removing the completed review from the estimate. Slice 120 is next, followed by Slices 121-124, Review 28, Slices 125-128, Slice 117, Review 29, and Slice 118. Slice 123 now means only the remaining signed COIN-M REST receive-window/read-model work; the six history-download receive windows are already complete. ## Slice 120: current parameterless COIN-M REST listen-key lifecycle

The three COIN-M REST User Data Stream operations were re-read on 2026-08-10 from the rendered current catalog rather than inferred from the previously corrected USDⓈ-M implementation. `POST`, `PUT`, and `DELETE /dapi/v1/listenKey` are API-key-only unsigned requests with no query or body parameters and IP weight 1. Start returns the active `listenKey` and extends an already-active stream for 60 minutes. Keepalive extends the configured API key's stream for 60 minutes and returns the refreshed `listenKey`. Close invalidates that stream and returns no data. The current guide independently states the same 60-minute lifecycle and `-1125` recreation behavior.

The combined derivatives changelog was also rechecked. Its 2024-04-19 entry adds the keepalive response only to USDⓈ-M `PUT /fapi/v1/listenKey` and `userDataStream.ping`; it contains no corresponding COIN-M REST lifecycle entry. That USDⓈ-M notice was therefore not used as cross-product proof. The current rendered COIN-M request and response schemas are the authority for this correction.

The wrapper's start operation was already current. Keepalive and close no longer accept or serialize a historical caller-supplied listen key, and both local weights fall from 2 to the published 1. Keepalive now deserializes and returns the refreshed key instead of discarding it behind a Boolean result. Close retains a Boolean success projection over the real empty 2xx response; the shared transport's null-safe empty-body handling from Slice 113 is exercised directly. These are intentional public breaking corrections: preserving overloads with the old parameter would continue advertising an unsupported financial-session contract.

README and gated console call sites now use the parameterless lifecycle. The three owned public XML links point to the exact current catalog anchors, reducing retired COIN-M hierarchy residue from 55 to 52. The remaining category and endpoint links belong to later market/read/order owners and remain scheduled for Slice 128 rather than being mechanically rewritten here.

Three focused no-network tests cover the exact `/dapi/v1/listenKey` path, POST/PUT/DELETE methods, absent query and body data, API-key header, unsigned authentication metadata, exact weight 1, both listen-key responses, and the empty close response. All 440 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three pre-existing warnings. ## Slice 121: complete current COIN-M REST kline and futures-data contracts

The complete rendered COIN-M REST Market Data catalog was re-read on 2026-08-10 for the eight owning operations rather than transferring USDⓈ-M or stream assumptions. `GET /dapi/v1/klines`, `/continuousKlines`, `/indexPriceKlines`, `/markPriceKlines`, and `/premiumIndexKlines` publish the same exact fifteen interval values: `1m`, `3m`, `5m`, `15m`, `30m`, `1h`, `2h`, `4h`, `6h`, `8h`, `12h`, `1d`, `3d`, `1w`, and `1M`. All five now require their symbol or pair and reject `1s`, blank identifiers, and undefined enum values before transport. The integration notice permits either a COIN-M or USDⓈ-M symbol/pair on these paths. Continuous-contract klines additionally accept only `PERPETUAL`, `CURRENT_QUARTER`, and `NEXT_QUARTER`.

All five kline operations now implement the published limit-weight boundaries exactly: `[1,100)` costs 1, `[100,500)` costs 2, `[500,1000]` costs 5, and values above 1000 cost 10; an omitted limit defaults to 500 and therefore costs 5. The previous `<= 100` and `<= 500` comparisons undercounted explicit limits 100 and 500. Kline, continuous-contract, index-price, and mark-price requests also enforce the documented maximum 200-day explicit start/end range and reject reverse ranges. Premium-index klines do not publish that request-range rule, so the wrapper deliberately does not invent it there.

The three futures-data operations have intentionally different closed contract-type sets. Open Interest Statistics and Taker Buy/Sell Volume accept `ALL` plus the same three concrete types; Basis accepts only the three concrete types. `ALL` is now represented by the shared mapped enum, but every request consumer of that enum was checked: COIN-M methods use operation-specific validation, existing USDⓈ-M continuous-kline and Basis methods reject it, and Futures stream validation retains its explicit product sets. The new sentinel therefore cannot leak into endpoints that do not publish it. All three operations require `pair` and accept only the current `5m`, `15m`, `30m`, `1h`, `2h`, `4h`, `6h`, `12h`, and `1d` periods. Historical aliases and undefined casts are rejected instead of serialized. The catalog's latest-30-day statement is an availability boundary, not a published maximum request-range rule, so no speculative 30-day client validation was added.

The response tuples were reconciled field by field rather than assumed to share one meaning. COIN-M regular and continuous klines define tuple index 9 as taker-buy contract volume, so the misleading public `TakerBuyQuoteVolume` property was replaced by `TakerBuyVolume`; index 10 remains taker-buy base-asset volume. Index-price and mark-price klines expose tuple index 8 as the number of basic data points and now return a dedicated `BinanceFuturesPriceKline` model carrying `DataCount`. Premium-index klines explicitly mark index 8 as ignored and continue to use the common base model, preventing a false field from leaking across response types. Open-interest, taker-volume, and Basis response fields were also covered by complete deserialization assertions.

The derivatives changelog records the 2020-07-22 introduction of the three futures-data routes and the 2020-12-30 DAPI kline weight changes, but it does not publish their current closed interval or contract-type sets. Those historical entries remain context only; the current rendered endpoint schemas are the authority for the validation domains. The later UM/CM integration notice broadens symbol compatibility across the five kline paths but does not add interval or contract-type values.

Eight owned public XML links now target the exact current Market Data anchors for the five kline families, Open Interest Statistics, Taker Buy/Sell Volume, and Basis. Retired COIN-M hierarchy residue falls from 52 to 44; unrelated Market Data endpoints remain with their later owning work and final Slice 128 residue pass.

Eleven new exact no-network tests enumerate all fifteen accepted kline intervals, reject `1s` and undefined values across all five kline methods, verify the exact limit-weight boundaries and default, prove the documented four-operation 200-day range boundary without inventing it for premium-index klines, enforce required identifiers and exact period values, enumerate the three continuous/Basis contract types, prove `ALL` serialization only for the two published futures-data operations, reject every other defined or undefined contract type before transport, and deserialize every transport-specific kline and futures-data response field. All 42 related COIN-M/USDⓈ-M/stream enum tests and all 451 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three pre-existing warnings. No live Binance request, market-data query, WebSocket connection, authenticated account operation, order, cancellation, listen-key mutation, or other mutation was sent.

## Slice 122: complete current COIN-M Income History contract

The complete rendered `GET /dapi/v1/income` Account operation was re-read on 2026-08-10. It remains a signed USER_DATA query with IP weight 20 and optional `symbol`, `incomeType`, inclusive `startTime`/`endTime`, int64 `page`, `limit`, and int64 `recvWindow`. The current page caps `limit` at 1000 with a default of 100 and caps `recvWindow` at 60,000 milliseconds. The wrapper already used the correct path, method, authentication, weight, date serialization, and full valid limit range; it lacked the current closed type domain, pagination, receive-window ceiling, and product-specific response shape.

COIN-M publishes exactly seven request values: `TRANSFER`, `WELCOME_BONUS`, `FUNDING_FEE`, `REALIZED_PNL`, `COMMISSION`, `INSURANCE_CLEAR`, and the official wire spelling `DELIVERED_SETTELMENT`. A dedicated `BinanceFuturesCoinIncomeType` now represents only those values and rejects undefined casts before transport. The existing shared `BinanceFuturesIncomeType` remains the richer USDⓈ-M domain and is no longer accepted by the COIN-M operation. This prevents USDⓈ-M-only income types from leaking into DAPI while preserving the independently current USDⓈ-M endpoint.

The request now serializes int64 `page` and accepts an int64 receive window through the current shared 60,000-millisecond validator, including configured defaults. `limit` remains `int` because every officially valid value is bounded by 1000; widening that parameter would add a breaking change without representing any additional valid request. Binance states that an explicit start/end interval cannot exceed one year but does not define whether this is a fixed 365-day duration or a calendar-year calculation. Consistent with the history-download decision preserved by Review 27, the public contract records the server boundary but the client does not invent a leap-year-sensitive preflight rule. A deterministic test deliberately sends the 366-day calendar year from 2024-01-01 through 2025-01-01 to prove that no unsupported fixed-day assumption was introduced.

The current COIN-M response defines `tranId` and `tradeId` as strings, whereas the shared USDⓈ-M response models `tranId` as int64. A dedicated `BinanceFuturesCoinIncomeHistory` now owns all eight published fields and preserves arbitrary string transaction identifiers. Returning this model, replacing arbitrary request strings with the closed enum, inserting `page`, and changing the receive-window parameter to int64 are intentional public breaking corrections under the project's current-contract-only policy. The default README and gated console calls require no source change because they use no arguments and infer the response type.

The derivatives changelog records only the 2023-08-14 addition of `page` to this COIN-M endpoint; it contains no current enum, identifier-type, range, or receive-window schema. The current endpoint page is therefore authoritative for those fields. The 2026 CM/UM integration notice does not list Income History among the routes that gained cross-product symbol support, so the wrapper does not generalize the catalog's isolated `BTCUSDT` example into undocumented cross-product behavior.

The owned public XML link now targets the exact current Account anchor, reducing retired COIN-M hierarchy residue from 44 to 43. Nine new deterministic cases cover all seven enum values bidirectionally, the full signed request including int64 page and receive window, weight 20, every response field, an arbitrary string transaction identifier, the ambiguous one-calendar-year boundary, undefined enum rejection, limit validation, and explicit/configured receive-window rejection. All 32 related COIN-M/USDⓈ-M account tests and all 460 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three pre-existing warnings. No live Binance request, authenticated account query, WebSocket connection, order, cancellation, listen-key mutation, or other mutation was sent.

## Slice 123: current COIN-M signed REST read contracts and receive-window ceilings

The rendered current COIN-M Account Information, Position Information, and Current All Open Orders operations were re-read on 2026-08-10 alongside the derivatives changelog and the effective CM/UM architecture-integration notice. Account Information remains signed USER_DATA `GET /dapi/v1/account` with IP weight 5. Position Information remains signed USER_DATA `GET /dapi/v1/positionRisk` with IP weight 1 and optional `marginAsset`/`pair` filters. Current All Open Orders remains signed USER_DATA `GET /dapi/v1/openOrders`, now publishes optional `symbol` and `pair`, and consumes IP weight 1 for a single symbol or 40 for multiple symbols. All three operations cap `recvWindow` at 60,000 milliseconds. The integration notice independently confirms that an invalid post-migration open-orders symbol now returns `-1121`; the wrapper does not preflight symbol existence from a stale local market snapshot.

The account response now matches the published top-level and nested position data. The current Account schema, rather than sparse historical changelog entries, is authoritative: the changelog records older position amount and update-time additions but does not enumerate today's complete response.

The REST Position Information response is no longer deserialized into the model shared with WebSocket `account.position`. A dedicated `BinanceFuturesCoinPositionRisk` carries the exact REST fields, including `unRealizedProfit` and `maxQty`, without falsely exposing the WebSocket-specific `notionalValue` or future `isolatedWallet` correction owned by Slice 127. Current All Open Orders now serializes its documented optional `pair` filter. Binance publishes no symbol/pair exclusion rule for this operation, so the wrapper does not invent one; its documented weight remains 1 whenever the request selects a single symbol and 40 for pair or unfiltered multi-symbol queries.

The shared 60,000-millisecond validator now guards the final twenty previously unguarded signed COIN-M REST receive-window call sites across Account and Trade, including configured defaults. This is a fail-before-transport safety correction only: Slice 123 does not alter normal-order type domains, mutation payload semantics, or acknowledgement models owned by Slices 124-125. A residue scan proves that no raw receive-window normalization remains in those two signed clients outside the validator itself.

## Slice 124: current COIN-M REST normal/Algo order migration

The rendered current COIN-M New Order and Place Multiple Orders contracts, the dated 2026-06-10 CM/UM architecture-integration notice effective by 2026-06-30, the combined derivatives changelog, and the current USDⓈ-M Algo endpoint schemas were re-read on 2026-08-10. The COIN-M normal endpoint schemas still enumerate historical conditional fields, but their newer inline migration note and the effective integration notice state that `POST /dapi/v1/order` and every item in `POST /dapi/v1/batchOrders` reject `STOP`, `STOP_MARKET`, `TAKE_PROFIT`, `TAKE_PROFIT_MARKET`, and `TRAILING_STOP_MARKET` with `-4120`. The same notice is the only current COIN-M source that publishes replacement `POST /dapi/v1/algoOrder`, `DELETE /dapi/v1/algoOrder`, and `GET /dapi/v1/openAlgoOrders` operations; both the rendered COIN-M Trade catalog and current generated connector omit those three routes. Route existence therefore comes from the effective COIN-M notice. Its explicit “same algo-order surface as UM” statement permits the current USDⓈ-M endpoint pages to supply the complete request, response, weight, and validation detail without inventing COIN-M-specific fields. The combined changelog contains no entry that enumerates these three COIN-M operations and was not treated as an exhaustive authority.

COIN-M REST `PlaceOrderAsync` and `PlaceOrdersAsync` now accept only `LIMIT` and `MARKET`; all five migrated stop types, response-only `LIQUIDATION`, and undefined enum casts fail before trade-rule evaluation or transport. The six conditional-only single-order parameters were removed. The shared batch request no longer exposes `StopPrice`, `ActivationPrice`, `CallbackRate`, `WorkingType`, or `PriceProtect`, because after this migration neither normal Futures batch endpoint has a valid consumer for them. This is an intentional public breaking correction under the current-contract-only policy, not a compatibility alias. Batch input is null-checked, materialized exactly once, constrained to one through five non-null items, and completely type-validated before trade-rule adjustment can mutate an earlier request. The normal DAPI form bodies contain no conditional fields and retain the published 60,000-millisecond receive-window ceiling and IP weights 0/5.

`PlaceAlgoOrderAsync` exposes only the five current conditional types and sends the one-value `algoType=CONDITIONAL` plus every supplied business field and timestamp in the signed form body to `POST /dapi/v1/algoOrder` with IP weight 0. It reuses the now product-neutral Futures Algo validator for enum domains, close-all and Hedge Mode conflicts, type-specific parameters, callback bounds, client IDs, response modes, self-trade prevention, price matching, and GTD bounds. The placement schema publishes no `recvWindow` maximum, so explicit 60,001 ms and configured 90,000 ms values remain valid; borrowing the 60,000 ms ceiling from another endpoint would be speculative.

`CancelAlgoOrderAsync` sends the documented int64 `algoId` and/or usable `clientAlgoId` alternatives in a signed DELETE query with IP weight 1. `GetOpenAlgoOrdersAsync` sends optional `symbol`, `algoType`, and int64 `algoId` filters in a signed GET query and reports IP weight 1 with a symbol or 40 without one. Both operations publish and enforce the 60,000-millisecond receive-window ceiling, including configured defaults, and reject blank supplied string filters before transport. The complete existing placement, cancellation, and open-order response models are shared by both Futures products; their product-specific summaries and the internal validator name were made neutral rather than cloning identical models.

README and the explicitly double-gated console now expose the three COIN-M Algo calls. No example executes without both the `--run-live-examples` argument and the exact interactive confirmation. The two owned normal-order XML links now target the exact current COIN-M Trade anchors; the three Algo methods link to the effective integration notice because Binance publishes no COIN-M endpoint pages for them. Retired COIN-M hierarchy residue falls from 40 to 38. Immediate order acknowledgement field separation remains explicitly owned by Slice 125, and COIN-M WebSocket normal-order migration remains owned by Slice 126; neither was silently folded into this REST slice.

Nineteen new deterministic COIN-M cases cover the complete normal/Algo public surfaces, both accepted and every rejected normal type, single-use batch enumeration, one-to-five/null/item guards, absence of historical conditional parameters, exact signed DAPI body/query placement, weights 0/1/5/40, complete placement and open-order response deserialization, both cancellation identifiers, shared fail-closed Algo validation, and endpoint-specific explicit/configured receive-window behavior. All 82 related USDⓈ-M/COIN-M REST and shared WebSocket validation tests and all 485 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three pre-existing warnings. No live Binance request, authenticated account query, WebSocket connection, order, cancellation, or other mutation was sent.

## Slice 125: exact COIN-M immediate order acknowledgement models

The current rendered COIN-M REST Trade catalog was re-read for `POST /dapi/v1/order`, `POST /dapi/v1/batchOrders`, `PUT /dapi/v1/order`, `PUT /dapi/v1/batchOrders`, `DELETE /dapi/v1/order`, and `DELETE /dapi/v1/batchOrders`. The current WebSocket Trade catalog was independently re-read for `order.place`, `order.modify`, and `order.cancel`. The effective architecture-integration notice explicitly removes `avgPrice` and COIN-M `cumBase` from all nine immediate placement, modification, and cancellation responses while retaining those fields on read operations. The endpoint schemas additionally confirm that immediate acknowledgements never publish the shared query model's USDⓈ-M-only `cumQuote`, creation time, or good-till date.

All nine mutation methods previously returned the shared `BinanceFuturesOrder`, so callers saw query-only fill and time properties that could never be populated by those wire responses. REST placement and cancellation now return `BinanceFuturesCoinRestOrderAcknowledgement`, while REST modification returns its derived `BinanceFuturesCoinRestOrderModificationAcknowledgement`; the WebSocket operations follow the same split with `BinanceFuturesCoinSocketOrderAcknowledgement` and its modification subtype. Both transport families derive from a transport-neutral acknowledgement base containing only the common published fields. The REST subtype alone carries its published `activatePrice`, `priceRate`, `priceMatch`, and `selfTradePreventionMode` fields, and only the modification subtypes expose the endpoint-specific `modifyId`. Batch REST error objects retain ordered per-item `CallResult` behavior through dedicated internal result subtypes. These return-type changes are intentional public breaking corrections under the current-contract-only policy.

`GetOrderAsync`, order-list/open-order queries, force-order queries, and every other read path continue to return `BinanceFuturesOrder`, including `avgPrice`, product-specific cumulative fill fields, creation time, and good-till date where their read schemas publish them. No field was deleted from the query model. Slice 125 does not modify WebSocket placement parameters or validation; the LIMIT/MARKET migration and int64 request corrections remain isolated in Slice 126.

Four new contract tests lock the absent query-only properties, REST-versus-WebSocket field split, all six REST public return signatures, all three WebSocket public return signatures, and preservation of the read-order surface. ## Slice 126: current COIN-M WebSocket API normal-order placement

The current rendered COIN-M WebSocket API Trade catalog and the effective [COIN-M / USDⓈ-M architecture-integration notice](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/products/derivatives-trading-coin-futures/Important-CM-UM-Integration-Notice) were re-read together. The rendered `order.place` parameter table is internally stale: its formal type enum and conditional rows still show STOP, STOP_MARKET, TAKE_PROFIT, TAKE_PROFIT_MARKET, and TRAILING_STOP_MARKET. The same current Trade page explicitly says those five types are rejected with `-4120`, and the effective integration notice names COIN-M WebSocket `order.place` as an affected normal-order operation that must route conditional orders to the DAPI REST Algo Order service. The official generated Python connector at HEAD `8d3256f` mirrors the stale formal table and therefore is corroborating implementation history, not authority over the effective migration behavior. No COIN-M-specific changelog entry publishes a more current replacement contract.

`IBinanceFuturesSocketClientCoinQueryTrade.PlaceOrderAsync` and its implementation now expose only the effective LIMIT/MARKET surface. The six historical conditional-only parameters (`stopPrice`, `activationPrice`, `callbackRate`, `workingType`, `closePosition`, and `priceProtect`) were removed rather than retained as nonfunctional compatibility aliases. `order.place` remains a signed `ws-dapi/v1` request with IP weight zero. Quantity and price are emitted as numeric JSON values instead of culture-formatted strings, boolean `reduceOnly` remains the documented lowercase wire value, and `recvWindow` is now int64 with the published 60,000-millisecond ceiling. An excessive explicit or configured receive window is rejected before trade-rule lookup or transport.

Fail-closed validation now requires a nonblank symbol, BUY/SELL side, positive quantity, the documented client-order-ID format, supported position side, ACK/RESULT response type, the four published self-trade-prevention modes, and COIN-M's exact GTC/IOC/FOK/GTX time-in-force domain. LIMIT requires time in force and exactly one of positive `price` or a non-NONE `priceMatch`; MARKET forbids price, price match, and time in force. The “exactly one” rule reconciles the stale table's mandatory-price cell with the same endpoint's explicit statement that `priceMatch` cannot be sent with `price`. `reduceOnly` is rejected whenever LONG or SHORT makes the request Hedge Mode, matching the published “cannot be sent” rule. USDⓈ-M-only GTD and RPI behavior was not copied into COIN-M.

The owned XML link now targets the exact current [COIN-M WebSocket API Trade `order.place` anchor](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-coin-m-futures/api/ws-api/trade#new-order), reducing retired COIN-M hierarchy residue from 38 to 37. ## Slice 127: current COIN-M WebSocket API account and signed-query contracts

The current rendered COIN-M WebSocket API [Account](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-coin-m-futures/api/ws-api/account) and [Trade](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-coin-m-futures/api/ws-api/trade) catalogs were re-read for `account.status`, `account.balance`, `order.cancel`, `order.status`, and `account.position`. All five remain signed requests on `ws-dapi/v1`; account, balance, and position have IP weight 5, while cancel and query-order have IP weight 1. The historical derivatives changelog does not provide a complete change history for these WebSocket API contracts. Its 2023-07-12 COIN-M `breakEvenPrice` entry names the REST account/position endpoints and the abbreviated user-data-stream event field, not these request/response operations, so the current endpoint schemas remain authoritative.

Every public `recvWindow` is now int64 and enforces the published 60,000-millisecond ceiling after applying the configured default, before signing or transport. Cancel and query-order serialize int64 `orderId` as a JSON number instead of a culture-formatted string, reject blank symbols and blank supplied client IDs, and require at least one documented order identity without inventing an exclusivity rule. Position Information no longer sends the undocumented `symbol` filter: its public surface and wire parameters now expose the documented optional `marginAsset` and `pair`, reject supplied blank values, and intentionally allow both because Binance publishes no mutual-exclusion rule. `receiveWindow` remains the first public position-query argument so an old positional symbol call cannot silently become a margin-asset filter; old positional symbol use now fails at compile time, while the new filters are intended as named arguments.

Response reconciliation found defects beyond the request changes. COIN-M account assets now use a product-specific model that does not expose USDⓈ-M-only `marginAvailable`; the USDⓈ-M public model remains unchanged. The account-position item now includes its published `isolatedWallet`. The WebSocket Position Information model now includes both missing `unRealizedProfit` and `isolatedWallet`. Query Order no longer returns the shared multi-transport `BinanceFuturesOrder`: its dedicated `BinanceFuturesCoinSocketOrder` preserves the published query fields, including average price, cumulative contract/base quantities, creation/update times, price match, and self-trade prevention, while removing five fields absent from this endpoint (`modifyId`, `cumQuote`, `activatePrice`, `priceRate`, and `goodTillDate`). ## Slice 117: current Options closing inventory

The current rendered Options [Account](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-options/api/rest-api/account), [Market Maker Block Trade](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-options/api/rest-api/market-maker-block-trade), [Market Data](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-options/api/rest-api/market-data), [Market Maker Endpoints](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-options/api/rest-api/market-maker-endpoints), [Trade](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-options/api/rest-api/trade), and REST [User Data Streams](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-options/api/rest-api/user-data-streams) catalogs were reconciled operation by operation. Their current counts are 2, 7, 12, 6, 14, and 3 respectively: 44 REST operations in total. The wrapper has all 44 current method/path pairs, no current operation is missing, and it still publishes four operations absent from both the current catalog and current official connector: `GET /eapi/v1/account`, `GET /eapi/v1/income/asyn`, `GET /eapi/v1/income/asyn/id`, and `GET /eapi/v1/historicalTrades`. The normalized route state is therefore 44 current/48 wrapper/44 exact/0 current-only/4 wrapper-only. Route equality is not semantic parity.

The rendered Options [Public](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-options/api/ws-streams/public) and [Market](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-options/api/ws-streams/market) stream catalogs publish five families each. The product-level [User Data Streams](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/products/derivatives-trading-options/user-data-streams) guide publishes six private events. There is no separate current Options WebSocket request/response API catalog; the official generated connector likewise exposes WebSocket Streams rather than a query/trading WebSocket API. The official Rust connector HEAD was independently pinned at `3b3f16969c30cc7b19265d5f41b79affc07e2b52`; its six REST modules, two stream modules, and generated models corroborate the 44-operation, 10-public/market-family, and six-private-event boundaries. The Options changelog was read through its current head; dated entries were used to locate affected contracts, never as a substitute for the endpoint schemas.

The closing inventory found current-contract defects rather than a clean surface:

- REST weight accounting is wrong for the all/single 24-hour ticker variants, order-book depth tiers, klines, kill-switch heartbeat, new order, cancel-multiple, cancel-all-by-underlying, current open orders, and the PUT/DELETE listen-key operations. Examples are operationally material: the wrapper records 5 instead of 40 when all tickers are requested, 1 instead of 10 for the heartbeat, 1 instead of 5 for batch cancellation, and 3 instead of 40 for unfiltered open orders.
- REST request drift includes the reversed `MMddyy` open-interest expiration format instead of `yyMMdd`, required symbol arguments where current endpoints make them optional, an optional account-trade symbol where the current contract requires it, missing limit validation, a five-order batch ceiling instead of ten, missing self-trade-prevention request fields, nullable quantities that are currently required, the undocumented open-orders `limit`, and `underlyings` instead of `underlying` on kill-switch configuration. Twenty-eight current signed operations still expose an int32 `receiveWindow`; only the already-audited Margin Account operation uses the documented int64 domain.
- REST response drift includes a structurally incorrect Exchange Information model and missing `contractType`, `underlyingType`, `nakedSell`, and `status`; `u` instead of `lastUpdateId` on the order book; conflated recent-trade identifiers; missing ticker `askPrice`; missing position `time`, `bidQuantity`, and `askQuantity`; stale position fields; missing order `source` and `selfTradePreventionMode`; the wrong exercise `side` field instead of `positionSide`; missing block-order creation time on operations that publish it; and loss of listen-key `expiration`. Cancel-all-by-underlying also deserializes the current `{code,msg}` acknowledgement as a nonexistent generic `data` value.
- REST listen-key keepalive and close still require and send `listenKey`, but the current PUT and DELETE contracts are parameterless API-key-only weight-one requests. Start returns both `listenKey` and int64 `expiration`; the wrapper discards expiration.
- Every Options stream subscription still uses the retired `wss://nbstream.binance.com/eoptions/stream` base rather than the current `wss://fstream.binance.com/{public|market}/stream` and private listen-key route. Current Public topics include `@optionTicker`, `@optionTrade`, book ticker, partial depth, and diff depth; the wrapper uses stale `@ticker`, `@trade`, old depth levels/speeds, and has no diff-depth or book-ticker surface. Current Market topics include `!index@arr`, `@kline_`, `@optionMarkPrice`, `!optionSymbol`, and `@openInterest@yyMMdd`; the wrapper uses stale individual index, `@markPrice`, `option_pair`, a missing open-interest separator, and the reversed date format. Existing stream models omit or mis-map substantial parts of every current payload.
- The private client dispatches only `ACCOUNT_UPDATE`, `ORDER_TRADE_UPDATE`, and `RISK_LEVEL_CHANGE`. It has no `BALANCE_POSITION_UPDATE`, `GREEK_UPDATE`, or `listenKeyExpired` callback/model/routing, and its existing account and order payloads target obsolete field layouts. The current documented private route is `wss://fstream.binance.com/private/ws/<listenKey>`, not the shared legacy route.
- Sixty-one Options XML references still use the retired `developers.binance.com/docs/derivatives/option` hierarchy; only four are already canonical. The repository has only three Options-specific test files and no deterministic Options stream or private-event coverage, so the current defects were not protected by regression tests.

This was an evidence-only inventory. No production API, public signature, response model, transport, sample behavior, or test behavior changed. All 508 deterministic tests pass, and a forced full multi-target solution build succeeds with zero errors and the same three pre-existing warnings. No Binance REST request, authenticated account query, WebSocket connection, order, cancellation, listen-key call, or other mutation was sent. ## Slice 131: current Options normal and batch order mutations

The six current Options [Trade](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-options/api/rest-api/trade) mutations were reconciled independently against their rendered endpoint schemas and exact DOM anchors: new order, place multiple orders, cancel one order, cancel multiple orders, cancel all by underlying, and cancel all by symbol. The official generated connector at pinned HEAD `3b3f16969c30cc7b19265d5f41b79affc07e2b52` resolved the rendered page's internal LIMIT-field conflict: quantity is required while price and time-in-force remain optional. The endpoint schemas and connector also agree on int64 receive windows, a ten-order placement batch, query parameters for all DELETE operations, and the exact 0/5/1/5/5/1 weight sequence. No incomplete changelog statement was treated as the contract.

Single placement now requires quantity, preserves Binance's default ACK response by omitting `newOrderRespType` unless requested, and exposes only the published ACK/RESULT response values plus NONE/EXPIRE_TAKER/EXPIRE_MAKER/EXPIRE_BOTH self-trade-prevention values. Batch requests require symbol, side, LIMIT type, and quantity at construction; accept one through ten orders; expose ACK/RESULT and the batch schema's three expiry modes; materialize caller enumerables once; and no longer mutate caller-owned request objects during trade-rule normalization. Both placement paths fail closed on shared-enum values that are invalid for Options. The published zero weight for single placement and weight five for batch placement are explicit.

All cancellation parameters now use the signed query rather than a DELETE body. Multiple cancellation materializes both identifier sequences once, rejects empty, oversized, null-item, and blank-client-ID inputs before transport, and uses JSON serialization instead of manually building an unescaped array. Its weight is corrected from one to five. Cancel-all-by-underlying now returns the exact int64-code acknowledgement and weight five instead of trying to deserialize a nonexistent nested data value; cancel-all-by-symbol preserves its string code and message instead of collapsing the response to `bool`. Both exact acknowledgement models remain product- and endpoint-specific.

The shared order response now carries `source` and typed `selfTradePreventionMode`; the cancel-one response's published `createDate` alias maps to the existing creation-time property without splitting read-model work into this mutation slice. All six public receive windows are int64 and reject explicit or configured values above 60,000 ms before transport. The old generic symbol validator was also removed from this touched surface because it rejects the hyphenated Options symbols published by Binance; Options mutations now enforce required nonblank identifiers without inventing a narrower undocumented symbol grammar.

The six owned XML references point to exact current mutation anchors, reducing retired Options documentation-root residue from 58 to 52. Eleven no-network tests lock request methods, paths, signing, body/query placement, exact weights, ten-order and single-enumeration behavior, escaped identifier arrays, enum rejection, int64 receive-window ceilings, current response fields, exact cancel-all acknowledgements, and the intentional breaking public quantity contracts. ## Slice 118: Spot FIX and SBE implementation execution contract

The current `Binance.FIX.Api` project was inspected before planning. It is an empty `Class1` packaged under a false “up-to-date, most-complete” description, references QuickFIX/n 1.14.0, and has no session, transport, authentication, message, decoder, public client, or test implementation. The solution has no SBE project or binary decoder. This slice does not disguise either absence as protocol progress and makes no runtime implementation change.

The current official [Spot FIX API](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/products/spot/fix-api), [SBE Market Data Streams](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/products/spot/sbe-market-data-streams), [SBE FAQ](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/products/spot/faqs/sbe_faq), [Spot User Data Stream](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/products/spot/user-data-stream), and official schema repository commit `b483413fcdf4da783cd3fcaad6fab7200a93297f` were reconciled. The program covers three TLS FIX roles, 25 distinct text FIX messages, ports 9000/9001/9002, 29 FIX SBE `1:1` templates, four independent market-stream `1:0` templates, and 92 general Spot SBE `3:5` templates. These are separate session/transport programs, not one decoder.

The schema audit found two upstream source defects that rule out blind `latest` automation. `spot_prod_latest.xml` still points to `spot_3_4.xml`, although the production lifecycle file and actual XML identify `3:5` as current. The FIX production lifecycle file also contains a trailing comma and is invalid strict JSON. The execution contract therefore pins explicit upstream filenames, commit, hashes, XML identities, and lifecycle evidence; any disagreement fails closed and requires human schema-promotion review.

Generator feasibility was tested rather than assumed. Real Logic/Aeron SbeTool has a maintained C# generator; release 1.39.0 is pinned, but it requires JDK 17 while this workstation currently exposes Java 8, so the generation toolchain is an explicit foundation slice. QuickFIX/n DDTool 1.14.1 parses each Binance dictionary but cannot combine them because both identify as `FIX44`, and generation writes Binance-specific fields into QuickFIX/n core source. A bounded generator-adapter compile gate now precedes all FIX order work.

The architecture is deliberately one-way: a new low-level `Binance.SBE.Api` owns versioned codecs, framing, schema registry, binary safety, and independent SBE market streams; `Binance.FIX.Api` owns the three FIX roles plus hybrid/full FIX SBE sessions and depends on the SBE layer; existing `Binance.Api` opts into general REST/WebSocket API/user-data SBE and maps it to current public domain models. Normal builds remain offline and Java-free because reviewed generated code is committed and regeneration only checks for a diff.

The financial-safety contract is non-negotiable. TLS/SNI/hostname validation is mandatory, only Ed25519 is accepted, raw API keys/signatures are redacted, role limits are local and observable, mutations are never replayed after an ambiguous disconnect, and pending commands become `UnknownDelivery` until reconciled. Binance does not support `ResendRequest`, while QuickFIX/n emits it automatically on a sequence gap; a deterministic “disconnect without sending ResendRequest” proof is therefore a release-blocking gate. If a contained adapter cannot prove it, development stops for an explicit engine/fork decision rather than weakening the behavior.

The complete package, schema, session, transport, testing, external-call, implementation-slice, forecast, and definition-of-done contract is in [FIX_SBE_EXECUTION_CONTRACT.md](FIX_SBE_EXECUTION_CONTRACT.md). It creates Slices 142-164 with Reviews 33-37: 23 development slices, five mandatory reviews, 28 minimum small turns, and an initial 146-292 active-hour envelope. That converts to roughly 3.7-7.3 weeks at 40 focused hours per week or 7.3-14.6 weeks at 20 hours per week; it is not a promised calendar date and excludes credential/authorization, Testnet availability, and upstream-blocker waits. Review 33 must re-estimate after the generator and QuickFIX/n safety spikes.

No production-code, public API, package, request, model, example, or test behavior changed. All 588 deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same three pre-existing warnings. No live Binance REST request, authenticated query, FIX/WebSocket connection, subscription, order, cancellation, or other mutation was sent. The original numbered remediation queue is now 141/141 complete, but the project is not complete: the evidence-backed FIX/SBE implementation queue begins at Slice 142.

## Slice 142: truthful non-packable FIX/SBE foundation

The empty FIX project no longer advertises a usable or complete multi-product wrapper. `Binance.FIX.Api` is explicitly development-stage, Spot-only, `IsPackable=false`, and `GeneratePackageOnBuild=false`; its broad Margin/Futures/Options package tags and placeholder public `Class1` were removed. QuickFIX/n Core moved from 1.14.0 to the current NuGet 1.14.1. The generic `QuickFIXn.FIX44` dependency was removed because the two official Binance custom FIX 4.4 dictionaries must own their generated field/message namespaces and factory under Slice 145.

The new non-packable `Binance.SBE.Api` foundation targets .NET Standard 2.0 and 2.1 so every existing `Binance.Api` target can consume it if the actual generated-code matrix passes Slice 144. It intentionally exposes no protocol API yet. Dedicated net10.0 `Binance.FIX.Api.Tests` and `Binance.SBE.Api.Tests` projects were added to the solution; their first regressions prevent a generic `Class1` placeholder from silently returning while allowing reviewed protocol types in later slices.

MSBuild property inspection proves both protocol projects have packing and package-on-build disabled. Real `dotnet pack` calls into separate empty temporary output directories produced zero files for both projects. Direct package inspection resolves only `QuickFIXn.Core` 1.14.1 for both FIX target frameworks, with no generic FIX44 dependency. Source scans find no production placeholder, old QuickFIX version, generic FIX44 package, or broad public FIX marketing claim.

Both new foundation tests and all 588 legacy tests pass, for 590 deterministic tests total. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and one remaining pre-existing console obsolescence warning; removing the two placeholder `Class1` documentation warnings is an intended result. No schema was vendored or generated, no protocol/session/transport behavior was added, and no live Binance request, authenticated query, FIX/WebSocket connection, subscription, order, cancellation, or other mutation was sent.

## Slice 143: license-safe Spot FIX schema lock and provenance

The official `binance-spot-api-docs` remote HEAD remains `b483413fcdf4da783cd3fcaad6fab7200a93297f`. The repository contains no `LICENSE`, `COPYING`, or `NOTICE` file, and the [Binance Terms](https://www.binance.com/en/terms) plus [Testnet Terms](https://www.binance.com/en/about-legal/terms-testnets) linked by its production/testnet terms pointers do not expressly grant redistribution of the raw schema artifacts. The project therefore does not vendor, pack, or claim redistribution rights over any Binance XML/JSON. This is a conservative repository decision rather than legal advice; generated output is also withheld from Git until Review 33 records an explicit repository-owner/licensing decision.

`eng/binance-spot-fix-schema-lock.json` pins only the seven FIX-owned inputs: the two text FIX dictionaries, FIX SBE schema `1:1`, production/testnet latest aliases, and both FIX lifecycle files. Every record owns its exact commit-pinned raw URI, byte length, SHA-256, and reviewed semantic identity. The lock records 19 Order Entry and 14 Market Data messages with 25 case-sensitive message types in their union, 29 FIX SBE messages, both aliases targeting `spot-fixsbe-1_1.xml`, and both lifecycle files reporting current schema `1:1` while remaining invalid strict JSON because of their upstream trailing commas.

This live raw-byte verification exposed and corrected an earlier provenance defect: Slice 118 had hashed Git checkout files after LF-to-CRLF conversion while describing them as hashes at the pinned commit. The contract now distinguishes exact raw transport bytes and corrects all seven previously listed raw hashes, including the independent/general SBE inventory rows. The FIX executable lock additionally includes the production/testnet aliases and both FIX lifecycle variants.

`eng/Download-BinanceSpotFixSchemas.ps1` is an explicit maintainer tool, not a build target. It accepts only the reviewed official repository/path set, requires a full commit hash and commit-pinned raw base URL, downloads all seven artifacts into memory, verifies all sizes and hashes, then verifies XML counts/identities, case-sensitive FIX message types, alias targets, and lifecycle state before creating or writing output. It refuses output inside the repository worktree and rejects existing destination files unless `-Force` is explicit. A real Windows PowerShell 5.1 run downloaded and verified 7/7 artifacts from the pinned commit, and a second run proved the default overwrite refusal. Normal restore, build, and tests remain network-free.

Three deterministic schema-lock tests embed the manifest without embedding upstream artifacts. Together with the Slice 142 foundation regression, all four focused FIX tests pass. The complete suite now contains 593 deterministic tests, and a forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same single pre-existing console obsolescence warning. ## Slice 144: pinned FIX SBE C# generation and compile feasibility

The official Aeron/SBE repository still identifies `1.39.0` as its latest stable release tag at commit `e773b57cac6b2008ce30dd219a33de49766c6013`; remote HEAD `44b04492d67aff2cd1fab19da77f365860b1e8c8` is the unreleased `1.40.0-SNAPSHOT`. The maintainer toolchain therefore remains pinned to stable `1.39.0`, not mutable HEAD. It locks the Apache-2.0 `sbe-all` executable jar, all seven matching C# runtime sources, and the runtime project's exact `System.Memory` 4.5.3 support assembly by official URI, byte length, and SHA-256.

The workstation still exposes only Oracle Java 8. Slice 144 does not mutate that installation: it pins the current Eclipse Temurin Windows x64 JDK `17.0.20+8` archive and `java.exe`, validates both hashes, and extracts the portable runtime only into an external maintainer cache. Work and cache paths inside the repository are rejected. The tool refuses an existing work directory rather than deleting or overwriting evidence.

The exact unchanged Binance FIX SBE `1:1` schema generated 84 C# files totaling 1,498,986 bytes with canonical manifest SHA-256 `91e6310676c8bbcb0b5978ee28577069abbf4ccac6e16f685ee6cf52c88710c5`. Two independent SbeTool runs produced identical paths, sizes, and hashes. Generation uses namespace directories and precedence checks, stops on parser errors, and accepts only one exact reviewed warning: `MarketDataIncrementalTrade.MDEntries` uses a `uint32 numInGroup`, while SbeTool recommends `uint8` or `uint16`. A missing, changed, or additional warning fails the pipeline.

An attempted standard-XSD gate exposed another upstream boundary rather than a wrapper defect: the Binance schema contains 56 `mbx:exponent` attributes referencing `Exponent`, `PriceExponent`, or `QtyExponent`; the unextended SBE XSD rejects those Binance vendor attributes. The final pipeline does not normalize, strip, or silently discard the original schema. It locks the 56 attributes separately, feeds the exact XML to SbeTool, and assigns decimal exponent mapping to the Slice 151 codec/model work.

SbeTool emits one `public` top-level type per generated file. Directly compiling that output into `Binance.SBE.Api` would publish 84 low-level wire types as accidental stable API. The feasibility pipeline therefore performs exactly 84 anchored top-level accessibility changes from `public` to `internal`, with no member or wire-code rewrite, and pins the resulting 1,499,154-byte manifest. The internalized generated sources plus seven matching internalized runtime sources compile with warnings-as-errors for `netstandard2.0`, `netstandard2.1`, `net8.0`, `net9.0`, and `net10.0`; restore uses an empty NuGet source set, and the resulting assembly exposes zero public types.

`eng/Test-BinanceSpotFixSbeGeneration.ps1` runs this complete gate only in an external work directory and writes a non-source result manifest there. The repository contains only the toolchain lock, maintainer script, and three offline lock regressions; it contains no Binance schema, generated codec, JDK, jar, SBE runtime source, or generated assembly. All four focused SBE tests and 596 complete deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same one pre-existing console obsolescence warning.

No Binance API/FIX/WebSocket connection, authentication, subscription, order, cancellation, or other mutation was sent. External reads were limited to the official Binance schema repository, Aeron/SBE GitHub and Maven Central artifacts, Eclipse Temurin release, and the pinned System.Memory NuGet package. Slice 145 is next and owns only the two text FIX dictionaries' generator-adapter compile decision. ## Slice 145: deterministic text FIX dictionary generation adapter

QuickFIX/n `v1.14.1` remains its latest official tag and `QuickFIXn.Core` 1.14.1 remains the latest published package. The release tag, source archive, NuGet package, embedded repository commit, license, and both net8.0/net10.0 Core assemblies are pinned by exact size and SHA-256. The package and source resolve to commit `44c111acdb253335fd3c035589795468ff60c400`. Neither the tool source nor the package binary is redistributed by this repository.

The unmodified dictionaries cannot be generated together by DDTool because both identify as `FIX44`. More importantly, DDTool's standard output path rewrites `QuickFIXn/Fields/Fields.cs` and `FieldTags.cs`; consuming that modified tree would silently turn the wrapper into a Core fork. The accepted bounded adapter adds only generation-time `customname` attributes, runs the official tool in disposable extracted source trees, verifies that those two files are its only Core-tree writes, and relocates the generated field catalog and role messages into Binance-owned internal namespaces. The published `QuickFIXn.Core` DLLs remain byte-identical inputs.

The current Order Entry dictionary contains 120 fields and 19 messages; Market Data contains 67 fields and 14 messages. Their union is 148 field names/tags with 39 shared definitions and no name/tag/type conflict. Generation produces two field files, 21 Order Entry sources, and 16 Market Data sources. Two independent runs produced the same 39-file raw manifest and the same 39-file adapted manifest. The adapter makes exactly 148 field-class, one tag-table, and 37 top-level message/factory accessibility changes, and owns separate `Binance.FIX.Api.Internal.OrderEntry` and `.MarketData` factories.

The adapted sources compile with warnings-as-errors against the exact unchanged Core assemblies for `net8.0`, `net9.0`, and `net10.0`. Reflection validates every case-sensitive message name and type, constructibility, both `IMessageFactory` implementations, a 205-type internal implementation surface, the exact QuickFix assembly version, and zero exported types. The verifier fails on any unexpected DDTool write, dictionary collision/inventory change, raw/adapted manifest drift, package/source hash change, compile warning, or reflected surface change.

`eng/Test-BinanceSpotFixDictionaryGeneration.ps1` performs the networked maintainer proof only in an external empty work directory and cache. Normal restore/build/test stays offline and embeds only the evidence lock. The repository still contains no Binance dictionary, generated FIX source, QuickFIX/n archive/binary, or generated assembly. QuickFIX/n's license does not settle rights over Binance-derived output, so Review 33 remains the explicit repository/package rights decision. This slice proves generation feasibility only; it does not prove authentication, TLS, sequence handling, no-resend behavior, transport, or any public client.

All seven focused FIX tests and 599 complete deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same one pre-existing console obsolescence warning. No Binance API/FIX/WebSocket connection, authentication, subscription, order, cancellation, or other mutation was sent. External reads were limited to the official Binance schema repository, QuickFIX/n GitHub source/tag data, and the official NuGet package feed. Review 33 is next. ## Backward Review 33: FIX/SBE foundation, generation, rights, and engine viability

Review 33 examined the complete `b4aa5ef..3ea34da` range: four commits, 20 repository files, 2,432 additions, and 43 deletions across protocol project boundaries, package guards, schema/toolchain locks, maintainer-only generators, deterministic tests, release notes, this contract, and the external tracking plan. Repository scans found no committed schema, generated source, generator/runtime archive, binary, assembly, or package artifact. Both protocol projects remain non-packable, normal restore/build/test remains offline, the published QuickFIX/n Core assembly remains unchanged, and no code, documentation, or execution-order defect was found in Slices 142-145.

The official [`binance-spot-api-docs`](https://github.com/binance/binance-spot-api-docs) repository HEAD remains `b483413fcdf4da783cd3fcaad6fab7200a93297f` and still returns no repository license. Its production terms file only points to the Binance Product Terms. Official Binance [Java](https://github.com/binance/binance-sbe-java-sample-app), [C++](https://github.com/binance/binance-sbe-cpp-sample-app), and [Rust](https://github.com/binance/binance-sbe-rust-sample-app) SBE sample repositories do publish their own generated general-Spot codec files under MIT licenses, but those licenses cover the files in those separate repositories; they do not expressly grant redistribution of C# output derived from the FIX dictionaries or FIX SBE schema. This review therefore makes a fail-closed repository decision, not legal advice: raw and generated Binance-derived artifacts remain outside Git and package output. Rights Gate 33A requires written license/permission or a documented legal review accepted by the repository owner before the generated-code chain starts; accepting uncertainty is not equivalent to obtaining third-party rights.

QuickFIX/n 1.14.1 remains technically viable. Its unchanged engine queues an inbound high sequence and constructs `ResendRequest`, then calls `IApplication.ToAdmin` before persisting or sending it. The new deterministic regression exercises a real initiator session from the published NuGet package: an inbound logon at `34=2` while expecting `34=1` produces an attempted `35=2`, `7=1`, `16=0`; the contained adapter disconnects through the registered session and throws `DoNotSend`. The responder records exactly one disconnect and zero sends, the message store remains empty, and sender/target sequence numbers both remain at one. No QuickFIX/n fork, reflection, source patch, live socket, or weakened sequence validation is involved.

The forward contract is narrowed rather than pretending the rights issue disappeared. Slice 146 is next and may implement only the common TLS/SNI/Ed25519/session/redaction/limit/cancellation core plus this exact no-resend behavior without committing generated artifacts. Rights Gate 33A then blocks Slice 147 and every later generated-code slice. After this review, 23 implementation/review turns and 130-260 active hours remain, but only Slice 146's 8-16 hours are currently actionable; the external rights wait has no honest calendar estimate. All eight focused FIX tests and 600 complete deterministic tests pass. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same one pre-existing console obsolescence warning. No Binance API/FIX/WebSocket connection, authentication, subscription, order, cancellation, or other mutation was sent.

## Slice 146a: immutable text FIX session contract

The 8-16 hour common session slice was too large for the agreed short-turn cadence, so its active-hour envelope is unchanged but execution is split into four independently verifiable turns: immutable endpoint/options ownership, Ed25519 logon and redaction, TLS/SNI transport projection, and lifecycle/budget/no-resend completion. This is a living-contract correction, not new protocol scope.

The current official production [FIX API](https://github.com/binance/binance-spot-api-docs/blob/master/fix-api.md) and [Spot Testnet FIX API](https://github.com/binance/binance-spot-api-docs/blob/master/testnet/fix-api.md) were refreshed at unchanged repository HEAD `b483413fcdf4da783cd3fcaad6fab7200a93297f`. The six text FIX endpoints remain the Order Entry, Drop Copy, and Market Data hosts under `binance.com` and `testnet.binance.vision`, all using mandatory `tcp+tls` port 9000. Both environments retain target component `SPOT`, the exact 1-8 character ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` SenderCompID domain, heartbeat interval 5-60 seconds, and the same message-handling and response-mode values.

`BinanceFixSessionOptions` now requires the caller to choose production or Spot Testnet explicitly; there is no implicit production default and no arbitrary host override. Environment and role resolve to one immutable official TLS URI. SenderCompID and heartbeat fail before any transport can exist. Undefined enum values fail closed. Sequential message handling is the shared wrapper default. Order Entry and Drop Copy default to complete execution-report delivery; unordered processing and acknowledgement-only delivery require explicit constructor arguments. The current Market Data dictionary does not contain `ResponseMode (25036)`, so Market Data exposes no response mode and rejects an explicit value instead of preparing an invalid Logon. All validated properties are getter-only. No API key, private key, certificate bypass, socket, QuickFIX session, reconnect loop, or order surface exists in this slice.

Twenty-six focused option cases and all 34 FIX tests pass; the complete deterministic suite contains 626 passing tests. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same one pre-existing console obsolescence warning. No live Binance request, FIX/WebSocket connection, DNS lookup, TLS handshake, authentication, subscription, order, cancellation, or other mutation was sent. Slice 146b is next. Twenty-five implementation/review turns and 128-257 active hours remain, but only Slices 146b-146d are actionable before Rights Gate 33A; its external wait remains unestimated.

## Slice 146b: Ed25519 Logon and credential boundary

The current official production [FIX API](https://github.com/binance/binance-spot-api-docs/blob/master/fix-api.md) and [Spot Testnet FIX API](https://github.com/binance/binance-spot-api-docs/blob/master/testnet/fix-api.md) were refreshed at unchanged repository HEAD `b483413fcdf4da783cd3fcaad6fab7200a93297f`. Both still require Ed25519-only credentials and define the signature as ASCII values of `35=A`, `49`, `56=SPOT`, `34`, and `52`, joined in that exact order with SOH, signed with Ed25519, and encoded as Base64 for `RawData (96)`. Required Logon body fields remain `95`, `96`, `98=0`, `108`, `141=Y`, `553`, and `25035`; `25036` is role/schema dependent and Drop Copy requires `9406=Y`.

`BinanceFixEd25519Credentials` accepts only a non-empty printable-ASCII API key plus an unencrypted PKCS#8 Ed25519 PEM byte span. It imports the key into NSec without retaining PEM text, clears temporary private/signature byte arrays, exposes no public key/signing getter, redacts `ToString`, rejects non-Ed25519 keys, and rejects all use after disposal. The internal Logon authenticator first removes every field it owns so a failed reauthentication cannot retain stale credentials, then refuses non-Logon, incomplete, mismatched, or malformed QuickFIX headers before adding new credential fields. It signs only the five actual header values and applies the exact shared and role-correct fields. The exact Binance published private-key/signature sample passes, including serialized `95=88` immediately before `96`. A structured redactor replaces only complete SOH-delimited `96` and `553` values, including repeats, without substring/tag-collision corruption. No raw logger or session exists yet, so no claim is made that arbitrary third-party logging is safe.

The FIX project reuses the solution's existing NSec.Cryptography dependency. Official NuGet metadata shows `25.4.0` is the latest stable release compatible with both required `net8.0` and `net9.0` targets; `26.4.0` supports `net9.0` but fails the required `net8.0` restore and was therefore rejected. The current official NuGet vulnerability feed reports no known vulnerable direct or transitive package in the FIX project. Eighteen focused authentication/redaction tests and all 52 FIX tests pass; the complete deterministic suite contains 644 passing tests. A forced full multi-target solution rebuild succeeds with zero errors and the same one pre-existing console obsolescence warning. Only official documentation/package metadata was read over HTTPS; no Binance FIX/API/WebSocket connection, account authentication, subscription, order, cancellation, or other mutation was sent. Slice 146c is next. Twenty-four implementation/review turns and 126-253 active hours remain, but only Slices 146c-146d are actionable before Rights Gate 33A; its external wait remains unestimated.
